# Knowledge index

One-line pointers to evergreen docs. Per-ticket implementation notes live under `codebase/` and aren't indexed here — that directory listing is its own index.

## Features

- [Splash screen](features/splash-screen.md) — system-managed cold-launch splash via `androidx.core:core-splashscreen:1.0.1` (#80); launcher `<activity android:theme="@style/Theme.PyrycodeMobile.SplashScreen">` resolves a `Theme.SplashScreen`-parented style (compat library, no `android:` namespace) with `windowSplashScreenBackground = @color/ic_launcher_background` (#FF32628D, reused from the launcher icon — no parallel color name) + `windowSplashScreenAnimatedIcon = @drawable/ic_splash_logo` (new 288×288dp viewport with all paths inside the inner 192dp safe area; single white-filled r=64 circle centred at 144,144 — placeholder, not brand identity) + `postSplashScreenTheme = @style/Theme.PyrycodeMobile`; `MainActivity.onCreate` calls `installSplashScreen()` as its **first** statement before `super.onCreate(...)` (AndroidX swaps the theme to `postSplashScreenTheme` before the window is realised — calling after `super` leaks the splash theme into the first app frame on some OEMs) with the returned `SplashScreen` handle intentionally discarded — no `setKeepOnScreenCondition { ... }`, no exit-animation listener (the [Navigation](features/navigation.md) #13 conditional start destination + `produceState`+empty-`Surface` placeholder already covers the only flash window). Application-level `android:theme` stays at `Theme.PyrycodeMobile` so any future non-launcher activity inherits the normal app theme. Splash drawables resolve **outside** the app theme so fills must be literal hex (`#FFFFFFFF`), not `?attr/...` references. No automated tests — splash is a system-window concern that `androidx.compose.ui.test` attaches after and `ActivityScenario` bypasses; verification surface is the build + one manual Android 13+ cold launch (Android 11/12 compat fallback is solid-background-no-animation-envelope, expected not a regression). Branded artwork + animated transition + any `windowSplashScreenAnimationDuration` / `windowSplashScreenIconBackgroundColor` items are a deliberate Phase 5 follow-up.
- [Navigation](features/navigation.md) — single-activity Compose NavHost in `MainActivity`; start destination gated on the encrypted `PairedServerStore` (`load() != null`, since #295; previously `AppPreferences.pairedServerExists`) via `produceState` (`welcome` fresh, `channel_list` once paired); destinations `welcome`, `scanner`, `channel_list`, `discussions` (#24 — reached from the channel-list inline Recent-discussions section's "See all discussions (N) →" link since #69, which replaced the #26 pill), `conversation_thread/{conversationId}`, `settings`, `archived_discussions` (#94 — reached from the Settings Storage section's "Archived discussions" row), `about` (#271 — reached from the Settings About-section "About" row; a static no-VM sub-screen, `composable(Routes.ABOUT) { AboutScreen(onBack = { navController.popBackStack() }) }`); route constants in colocated `Routes` object. The `license` route (#91 — Settings About-section License row → in-app viewer) was deleted in #163 along with `LicenseScreen` once the Settings entry point disappeared; #271's `about` route is the About section's structural successor to it.
- [Welcome screen](features/welcome-screen.md) — first onboarding screen; stateless Composable with two navigation callbacks, mounted at the `welcome` route. Body locked to Figma node `6:32` since #57 — layered `Box` (solid `surface` + `Modifier.drawBehind` radial-gradient atmospheric overlay + content `Column(SpaceBetween)`); hero pairs `ic_pyry_logo` over `headlineLarge` title + `titleMedium` tagline + `bodyLarge` paragraph; CTA stack is filled `Button` w/ `ic_qr_scan_frame` leading icon over `TextButton` over centred `labelSmall` open-source footer. Pill polish landed in #149 — primary CTA carries `shape = RoundedCornerShape(28.dp)` on the existing 56dp height so the silhouette reads as a full pill (corner = height / 2, Figma node `6:41`'s `rounded-[28px]`); hero `Column`'s top padding is `168.dp` (Figma node `6:32`'s `pt-[168px]`, applied *after* `systemBarsPadding()` per the project convention to read Figma top values as "below the safe area"); secondary `TextButton` deliberately gets no shape override since the M3 text-button surface has no fill. Logo geometry repair landed in #150 — `ic_pyry_logo.xml`'s six arm `pathData` strings rewritten so each shaft starts at the canvas-centre pivot and extends outward (the buggy shafts shipped in #57 straddled the pivot, so opposite-rotation arms overlapped and inner branches tangled with the centre cursor — rendered as a knot at centre, not a snowflake); branch forks moved into the outer half of the shaft so the Y-spike-at-tip pattern from Figma node `9:4` reads. Post-#150 the Figma source-of-truth was re-authored (node moved from `9:2` to `80:2` "pyrycode-mark 1", encoding shape switched from six stroked arm groups + centre rectangle to a single filled continuous path); #167 rewrote `ic_pyry_logo.xml` end-to-end to mirror the new source — one `<path android:fillColor="#FFFFFF">` with viewport `91.002 × 103.812` at intrinsic `92dp × 104dp` (was `104 × 104` at `104dp × 104dp`) — and switched the Welcome `Icon` bounding box from `Modifier.size(104.dp)` to `Modifier.size(width = 92.dp, height = 104.dp)` so the new 92:104 drawable's left edge still aligns with the column's 32dp horizontal padding under default `ContentScale.Fit` (a square box would inner-pad the logo ~6.5dp inboard). Post-#167 Figma was edited again to bind `M3/Elevation Light/3` to the Hero group `6:33` (a two-layer black-with-alpha drop-shadow); #168 lifts that onto the Welcome logo `Icon` via `Modifier.shadow(elevation = 6.dp, shape = RectangleShape, clip = false)` chained **before** `.size(width = 92.dp, height = 104.dp)` (modifier order matters — outside-in for layout; reversing draws the shadow over a zero-size wrapper) — the framework synthesises the M3 Level 3 two-tier key+ambient shadow from the single 6.dp value, default `ambientColor` / `spotColor` = `DefaultShadowColor` (`Color.Black`) matches Figma's `#000000`-with-alpha base, and M3's `ColorScheme` has no `shadow` token so the framework default is the canonical answer (no new `MaterialTheme` slot needed to satisfy AC3). Compose API limitation: the shadow's outline is the `shape` parameter's geometry (here a 92×104dp rectangle), **not** the snowflake's alpha silhouette — a faithful silhouette shadow would need `GenericShape` parsing or `graphicsLayer { renderEffect = BlurEffect(...) }`, both disproportionate to a one-modifier ticket and judged tolerable on the dark canonical surface. Scope is the logo `Icon` only (not the Hero group) — the three Hero `Text` composables (title / subtitle / body) carry no `TextStyle(shadow = Shadow(...))` because their `onSurface` / `onSurfaceVariant` glyphs lack the luminance contrast for a black-with-alpha shadow to read on dark theme; per-glyph text shadow is a possible follow-up only if reviewer judgement demands it. `Icon(tint = MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary)` rendering pathway preserved across both encodings; `#FFFFFF` tinting-bed convention preserved; the Figma SVG export's `fill="var(--fill-0, #7AB8E8)"` fallback was discarded because `Icon`'s `BlendMode.SrcIn` overrides per-pixel colour at composition time anyway. `Icon(tint = MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary)` rendering pathway preserved — the ticket-body framing that the defect was "single-tint silhouette / differentiated stroke vs fill colour" was misdiagnosed (Figma node `9:2`'s `get_variable_defs` returns only `Schemes/Primary`; the differentiation is shape-primitive — strokes for arms, fill for cursor — not colour). Instrumented Compose tests since #100 — four-method `WelcomeScreenTest` at `app/src/androidTest/.../onboarding/WelcomeScreenTest.kt` covers per-CTA `assertIsDisplayed()` plus per-CTA click-wiring with local `var pairedCount = 0` / `var setupCount = 0` counters (raw-callback shape — the screen's `(onPaired, onSetup)` signature predates the project-wide `(state, onEvent)` MVI shape, so the click-capture pattern degrades from `mutableListOf<Event>::add` to plain counters). CTA labels asserted as English literals (`"I already have pyrycode"` / `"Set up pyrycode first"`) because the production source carries them as literals too — no `strings.xml` entry to route through; the #149 shape + padding edits left those text nodes byte-identical, so tests passed unchanged.
- [Scanner screen](features/scanner-screen.md) — QR-pairing screen at the `scanner` route; viewport locked to Figma node `13:2` since #60 (scan-line shadow re-grounded in #121). **Stateful since #326, live camera since #334**: `ScannerScreen` is a thin stateless `when(state)` renderer over sealed `ScannerUiState` (`PermissionRequesting`/`ReadyToScan`/`Denied`/`Error(message)`/`Decoded(payload)`/`AwaitingConfirm(fingerprint, server)` since #343); the route owns a pure-synchronous `ScannerViewModel` (`MutableStateFlow(PermissionRequesting)` + `asStateFlow()`, `onEvent` maps `PermissionGranted->ReadyToScan`/`PermissionDenied->Denied`/`CameraError(m)->Error(m)`/`QrDecoded(p)->Decoded(p)`/`PairingPrepared(fp,server)->AwaitingConfirm` + `DeclinePairing->ReadyToScan` (#343), no `viewModelScope`/Android types) plus the runtime camera-permission flow (`rememberLauncherForActivityResult(RequestPermission())`, no accompanist; `LaunchedEffect(Unit)` checks `checkSelfPermission` first else launches once behind a `rememberSaveable requested` guard). `PermissionRequesting`/`ReadyToScan`/`is Decoded` -> `private ScannerViewport(onNavigateBack, onPasteCode, cameraPreview, modifier)` — the locked M3 `TopAppBar("Pair with pyrycode")` over a viewport `Box(weight(1f))` whose child stack is (back-to-front) **`cameraPreview()` live feed (ReadyToScan only)** + dual radial gradients moved into a `matchParentSize().drawBehind` child (so they layer OVER the camera — the `Box.drawBehind` paints behind ALL children; AC1) + `Canvas` stripe overlay every 7dp + 248dp four-corner `Reticle` (scan line = `Canvas` `BlurMaskFilter(12dp, NORMAL)` shadow via `drawIntoCanvas{nativeCanvas.drawRect}` under a crisp 2dp `primary` line) + `HintCard` AnnotatedString `pyry pair` `FontFamily.Monospace` accent, over a `Trouble scanning?` `TextButton`; **#334 changed no overlay pixels, only what fills behind it**. `Denied` -> existing `ScannerDeniedScreen` (#61) in-route; `Error` -> `private ScannerErrorContent` (message + clickable "Paste the pairing code instead"); `AwaitingConfirm` -> full-screen `private PairingConfirmContent` (fp verbatim + Confirm/Decline, #343). #334 rewired the trigger tap->decode: the whole-surface `detectTapGestures` was removed; back-arrow -> `onNavigateBack` (`popBackStack` to Welcome), paste -> `onPasteCode`; a real decode -> `Decoded` -> route `LaunchedEffect(state)` which (since #320) runs `parsePairingPayload` and (since #343) derives the fingerprint (`serverKeyFingerprint`, #342) and parks in `AwaitingConfirm` rather than persisting; the ONLY scan-path `PairedServerStore.save` is the gated `confirmPairAndNavigate` (the [Pairing confirm gate](features/pairing-confirm-gate.md)), `popUpTo(SCANNER){inclusive=true}` prevents re-fire. The camera lives in the route via a `cameraPreview: @Composable ()->Unit = {}` slot gated on `ReadyToScan` (`modifier` precedes the slot — Compose-lint ordering) injecting [Camera preview](features/camera-preview.md); bind failure -> `onCameraError(fixed const)` -> `CameraError` -> `Error` (no blank viewport, AC4). Manifest `CAMERA` + non-required `camera.any` (#326, unchanged); Koin `viewModel { ScannerViewModel() }`. Tests: 7 VM transition unit tests (`ScannerViewModelTest`, plain JUnit) + 13-method instrumented `ScannerScreenTest` (all `onTap`->`onNavigateBack`; originals prove AC1/AC3 no-regression of the locked overlay; adds `permissionRequesting_rendersViewportShell`/`denied_rendersScannerDeniedScreen`/`error_rendersMessageAndClickablePasteFallback`/`decoded_rendersViewportUnchanged`/`cameraPreviewSlot_rendersBehindLockedOverlay` which pins the slot behind the overlay with a fake `testTag("camera")` and no real camera). `CameraPreview` itself un-unit-tested (needs a device). Known NIT (deferred): on-resume permission re-check + process-death `Denied`->shell fallback. The decoded payload is untrusted external input — bare `String`, redacted `toString()`, parsed into a real `PairedServer` (#320) and identity-confirmed via its fingerprint before persisting (#343).
- [Camera preview](features/camera-preview.md) — the live-camera composable of the pairing scanner (#334): `CameraPreview(onQrDecoded, onCameraError, modifier)` renders a CameraX `Preview` via `AndroidView`+`PreviewView` with **`ImplementationMode.COMPATIBLE`** (TextureView — composites in-hierarchy so it honours the parent `clip(RoundedCornerShape(24.dp))` + the over-drawn atmosphere/reticle overlay; the default `PERFORMANCE`/SurfaceView lives in a separate window and punches through — AC1) **behind** the locked [Scanner screen](features/scanner-screen.md) viewport, and binds a parallel `ImageAnalysis(STRATEGY_KEEP_ONLY_LATEST).apply{setAnalyzer(bgExecutor, analyzer)}` feeding a fresh `remember { QrCodeAnalyzer(...) }` (resets the once-per-scan latch per scanner entry — the re-scan reset #333 deferred). In `DisposableEffect(Unit)`: `ProcessCameraProvider.getInstance(context)`, on its `getMainExecutor` listener build + `unbindAll()` + `bindToLifecycle(LocalLifecycleOwner, DEFAULT_BACK_CAMERA, preview, imageAnalysis)` (ties camera start/stop to the lifecycle, AC2), wrapped `try/catch(_:Exception){ onCameraError(CAMERA_BIND_ERROR_MESSAGE) }` (AC4 — a fixed private const, the exception is never interpolated so no device/stack leak); `onDispose{ unbindAll(); analysisExecutor.shutdown() }`. `analysisExecutor = Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor()` is **off-main** (required — `Tasks.await` in `MlKitQrFrameDecoder` blocks/throws on main, #333); the provider-future listener runs on main but only builds/binds (never `Tasks.await`). `onQrDecoded`/`onCameraError` wrapped in `rememberUpdatedState` so the long-lived analyzer/listener never call a stale route lambda. `LocalLifecycleOwner` imported from `androidx.lifecycle.compose` (not the deprecated `compose.ui.platform` one — `lint{abortOnError}`). New deps `androidx.camera:camera-view`/`camera-lifecycle`/`camera-camera2` pinned to `cameraCore = 1.4.2` (camera2 = Camera2 runtime backend; no directly-used API but MUST be on the classpath or `getInstance` finds no camera implementation). All UI-layer (no `data/`/DI/manifest change); the route injects it through the scanner's `cameraPreview` slot gated on `ReadyToScan`. Not unit-tested by design (needs a physical camera / `ProcessCameraProvider`). The decoded string is never logged/displayed; the pairing trigger moved tap->decode. Downstream: parse -> #320, fingerprint -> #342, confirm gate -> #343.
- [Scanner Denied screen](features/scanner-denied-screen.md) — stateless camera-permission-denied surface locked to Figma node `32:2` (#61); `Surface` + `Column` with a 120dp Compose-`Canvas` camera-with-strike illustration (rounded-rect body + viewfinder-bridge `Path` + stroked outer-lens circle + filled inner-lens dot + `colorScheme.error` diagonal `drawLine`), `headlineSmall` headline, `bodyMedium` explainer (`widthIn` 300dp), bottom-pinned `fillMaxWidth` filled `Button` + `TextButton` via a `Spacer(weight=1f)`. Two caller-owned lambdas. **Wired live in #326** as the `ScannerUiState.Denied` render inside `composable(Routes.SCANNER)`'s `when(state)` — reused as-is (no `TopAppBar` added, no new route): the route supplies `onOpenSettings` (the `ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS` intent) and `onPasteCode` (the shared `stubPairAndNavigate`). Instrumented Compose tests since #101 — three-method `ScannerDeniedScreenTest` at `app/src/androidTest/.../onboarding/ScannerDeniedScreenTest.kt` asserts the `"Camera permission required"` heading (exact match) plus `hasClickAction()` on `"Open settings"` and `"Paste code instead"`; structure only, the two lambdas are passed as `{}` no-ops and callback wiring isn't asserted.
- [Scanner Connecting screen](features/scanner-connecting-screen.md) — stateless in-flight-pairing surface locked to Figma node `32:20` (#62, re-aligned to the TopAppBar variant in #122); since #122 the public signature is `ScannerConnectingScreen(serverAddress, onBack, modifier)` with `onBack` required (no default — matches `SettingsScreen` / `ArchivedDiscussionsScreen` precedent; `LicenseScreen` was a third precedent until #163 deleted it), and the layout is `Surface(colorScheme.surface)` + `Column(systemBarsPadding)` containing the `TopAppBar` ("Pair with pyrycode" `titleLarge` on `onSurface`, transparent container, back-arrow `IconButton` with English-literal `contentDescription = "Back"` per `ScannerScreen.kt`'s pairing-flow grouping — `R.string.cd_back` is reserved for the back-nav-screen group: `SettingsScreen`, `DiscussionListScreen`, `ArchivedDiscussionsScreen`) over a weighted inner `Column(weight = 1f, fillMaxWidth, horizontalAlignment = CenterHorizontally)` whose vertical distribution is `Spacer(weight = 316f) → CircularProgressIndicator(size = 48.dp) → Spacer(28.dp) → bodyLarge headline ("Connecting to your pyrycode server…") on colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant (#122 corrected from onSurface) → Spacer(16.dp) → bodyMedium server address on colorScheme.onSurfaceVariant with fontFamily = FontFamily.Monospace as a top-level Text parameter (family-only override of the named role) → Spacer(weight = 372f)`. The 316:372 ratio is Figma's `(384 − 68) : (892 − 520)` computed against the content area below the 68dp TopAppBar so the spinner position survives different device heights (AC2 — proportional, not fixed dp); the 28dp/16dp intra-cluster gaps stay fixed because Figma specifies pixel-accurate spacing there. Indicator still ships zero theming args (M3 defaults bind `colorScheme.primary` arc + `colorScheme.surfaceVariant` track). Still no NavHost wiring (no `Routes.SCANNER_CONNECTING` today; `onBack` is the forward-compatibility hook — Phase 4 caller supplies `{ navController.popBackStack() }` matching `MainActivity.kt:209/215/225`). Instrumented Compose tests since #101 — four-method `ScannerConnectingScreenTest` at `app/src/androidTest/.../onboarding/ScannerConnectingScreenTest.kt` asserts `hasText("Connecting to your pyrycode server", substring = true)` (substring deliberately avoids the U+2026 ellipsis), exact match on the `"home.lan:7117"` literal passed as `serverAddress`, and since #122 `topBarTitle_renders` (exact-match `"Pair with pyrycode"` regression guard) and `backButton_invokesOnBack` (`hasContentDescription("Back") → performClick() → assertTrue(backInvoked)`); no spinner-position pixel asserts — proportional layout is visually verified via `@Preview` review against Figma 32:20. The #62 "TopAppBar is a NavHost concern" rule was walked back in #122 — see [`codebase/122.md`](codebase/122.md) for the corrected rule (a TopAppBar shown in the screen's Figma frame goes in the screen, not the host).
- [QR code analyzer](features/qr-code-analyzer.md) — the **decode core** of the pairing scanner (#333): a CameraX `ImageAnalysis.Analyzer` (`QrCodeAnalyzer`) that decodes a frame to a QR string and surfaces it **exactly once per scan** via a lock-free `AtomicBoolean.compareAndSet(false, true)` latch, feeding the [Scanner screen](features/scanner-screen.md)'s `ScannerViewModel` through a `QrDecoded` event -> `Decoded(payload)` state (the success counterpart to #326's `CameraError`/`Error`). Decode is behind an injected `fun interface FrameDecoder { fun decode(ImageProxy): String? }` seam — production `MlKitQrFrameDecoder` (bundled `com.google.mlkit:barcode-scanning:17.3.0`, QR-only `BarcodeScanning.getClient`, `Tasks.await(scanner.process(InputImage.fromMediaImage(mediaImage, rotationDegrees))).firstOrNull { it.rawValue != null }?.rawValue`, `@ExperimentalGetImage`, returns `null`/logs-nothing on no-QR/failure) vs. test fakes returning canned payloads, so the debounce/dispatch logic is JVM-tested with no device (`QrCodeAnalyzerTest`). `analyze()` closes the `ImageProxy` in `finally` (CameraX won't deliver the next frame otherwise); the latch never resets within an instance — re-scan is a fresh analyzer per scanner entry. The decoded string is **untrusted external input** surfaced as a bare `String` (deliberately not a "validated" wrapper) — never parsed/persisted/displayed/logged; AC5 no-log is belt-and-suspenders (the stochastic no-`Log.*`-references-`payload` rule + the deterministic redacting `toString()` on `Decoded`/`QrDecoded`, `<redacted N chars>`, doesn't touch `equals`/`hashCode`). Deps `androidx.camera:camera-core:1.4.2` (`ImageAnalysis.Analyzer` + `ImageProxy`) + bundled ML Kit (offline-first, no model-download MITM surface). **Live since #334** — instantiated in production by [Camera preview](features/camera-preview.md), bound to a live CameraX preview on a **background** executor (`Tasks.await` blocks/throws on main); `Decoded` renders the existing viewport unchanged (scope guard, payload never displayed). Parse -> #320; fingerprint -> #342; confirm gate -> #343. Server-side `Encode`/`Decode` analog: pyrycode #211/#212.
- [Pairing payload parser](features/pairing-payload-parser.md) — the **untrusted→trusted boundary** of pairing (#320, `data/network/PairingPayloadParser.kt`): `parsePairingPayload(scanned)` decodes the **outer** base64url-no-pad QR wrapper (Go `RawURLEncoding`; the **two-alphabet trap** — the inner `server_static_pubkey` stays base64-std, decoded by `decodeServerStaticPubkey`), deserializes [`QrPayload`](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) via `MobileJson`, validates every field (non-empty `server`/`relay`/`token`; relay = ws(s) origin with host via `java.net.URI`, not `android.net.Uri`/OkHttp; 32-byte base64-std pubkey; **EOF after the JSON object** so trailing data is rejected while extra keys are tolerated), and maps to [`PairedServer`](features/paired-server-store.md). Pure, synchronous, `data/`-portable, **never throws** — returns `PairingParseResult.Success(server)` / `Failure(reason)` where `reason` is a fixed byte-safe category label (never a field value; the parser does no logging). `MainActivity`'s `Decoded` `LaunchedEffect` no longer persists `Success` (since #343): it derives the fingerprint via the sibling `serverKeyFingerprint` and parks in `AwaitingConfirm`; the only scan-path `PairedServerStore.save` is gated behind the confirm (the [Pairing confirm gate](features/pairing-confirm-gate.md)). Parse/derive/save failures route through `ScannerEvent.PairingFailed → ScannerUiState.Error`. Replaces the #295 stub write on the scanned path (the #295 stub was removed in #489; the manual paste path now routes through the same fingerprint gate via the store-free [Paste code dialog](features/paste-code-dialog.md) — #501, not a stub). New helper `decodeBase64UrlNoPad` lives in `MobileWireCodec.kt`.
- [Static-key fingerprint](features/static-key-fingerprint.md) — the **mobile half of the QR-pairing MITM visual check** (#342, `data/crypto/StaticKeyFingerprint.kt`): pure `fun staticKeyFingerprint(staticKey: ByteArray): String` derives the fingerprint the user reads against the desktop's `Static-key fp:` line. **Byte-for-byte parity with pyrycode#432** `Fingerprint([32]byte) string` is the whole contract — the full **256-bit** BLAKE2s digest of the 32-byte X25519 static pubkey (vendored [`Blake2sMessageDigest`](decisions/0004-vendor-noise-java-crypto.md), constructed **directly** — not `MessageDigest.getInstance("BLAKE2S-256")`, which `NoSuchAlgorithmException`s with no JCA provider on Android), truncated to the **first 8 bytes** (64-bit width is load-bearing security — a 32-bit fp is brute-forceable; never narrow to 4), rendered colon-separated lowercase hex (exactly 23 chars, `^[0-9a-f]{2}(:[0-9a-f]{2}){7}$`). **Full-digest-then-truncate, never a configured-short "BLAKE2s-64"** (which folds output length into the init block → a different digest `a8:b3:…`, not the pinned `32:0b:…`; the vendored class is fixed-256-bit by construction so the trap can't bite — the note guards a future variable-length swap). Renders via Kotlin 2.2 `digest.toHexString(0, 8, HexFormat { bytes.byteSeparator = ":" })` — lowercase, byte-width-correct, sidesteps the signed-`Byte` sign-extension trap (`"%02x".format(b)` → `ffffffa9` without `and 0xFF`), no `@OptIn`. Input is the **raw 32-byte `ByteArray`** (`require(size == 32)`, fixed no-key-byte message); the base64-std→bytes decode stays in `data/network` (the [confirm gate](features/pairing-confirm-gate.md)'s job since #343, via `serverKeyFingerprint`, mirroring `NoiseSessionFactory.create()`:30-40). Pure, synchronous, no I/O, no logging, stateless/thread-safe; `data/`-portable (only `com.southernstorm.noise.*`). Pinned zero-vector test asserts the **hard-coded literal** `32:0b:5e:a9:9e:65:3b:c2` (never recomputed in-test). Split from #321 (the [Pairing confirm gate](features/pairing-confirm-gate.md), #343, consumes this between the [parser](features/pairing-payload-parser.md)'s `Success` and `PairedServerStore.save`); reuses [ADR 0004](decisions/0004-vendor-noise-java-crypto.md)'s BLAKE2s.
- [Pairing confirm gate](features/pairing-confirm-gate.md) — the **security checkpoint** of QR-pairing (#343): interposes a human confirm between the [parser](features/pairing-payload-parser.md)'s `Success` (#320) and the persist — structural QR validity != authenticity, so an attacker's well-formed QR would persist identically without it. On `Success` the `MainActivity` `Decoded` `LaunchedEffect` no longer saves: it derives the [static-key fingerprint](features/static-key-fingerprint.md) via the new pure `fun serverKeyFingerprint(staticKeyBase64): String?` (in `PairingPayloadParser.kt`: base64StdDecode -> re-validate 32 bytes -> `staticKeyFingerprint`; **`null`-not-throws** so `staticKeyFingerprint`'s `require` can't crash the coroutine — mirrors `NoiseSessionFactory.create`) and fires `ScannerEvent.PairingPrepared(fp, server)` -> new `ScannerUiState.AwaitingConfirm(fingerprint, server)`. `ScannerScreen` renders a full-screen `private PairingConfirmContent(fingerprint, onConfirm, onDecline, modifier)` (peer of `ScannerErrorContent`): the fp **verbatim** colon-lowercase-hex in `FontFamily.Monospace` inside a `SelectionContainer` with `contentDescription="Server fingerprint $fp"`, compare copy (vs the desktop `Static-key fp:` line), Confirm `Button` + Decline `OutlinedButton` each `fillMaxWidth().heightIn(min=48.dp)` — receives only the fp string + callbacks, **never the token-bearing `server`**. The ONLY scan-path `save` is the route-scope `confirmPairAndNavigate` lambda wired to `onConfirmPairing` (reads `(state as? AwaitingConfirm)?.server` — a save can't fire after the gate closed, and it persists exactly the record the fp was derived from: no shown-vs-saved TOCTOU; fp<->record are two fields of one immutable state). Decline button + `BackHandler(enabled = state is AwaitingConfirm)` -> `DeclinePairing` -> `ReadyToScan`, **nothing persisted**. Confirm is deliberately NOT a VM event (suspend save + nav stays a callback, VM stays Android-free); double-confirm benign (idempotent overwrite + `launchSingleTop`/`popUpTo`), no `Saving` state. Design-later (no Figma confirm surface across 599 nodes — non-decorative M3, visual retrofit owed). Tapjacking/overlay named OUT OF SCOPE (Activity-window obscured-touch filtering = follow-up); paste path now routes through this same gate too (#501, via the store-free [Paste code dialog](features/paste-code-dialog.md)). Tests: VM `PairingPrepared`/`DeclinePairing`, `serverKeyFingerprint` valid(pinned `32:0b:5e:a9:9e:65:3b:c2`)/wrong-len/non-base64/parse->derive seam, 5 `ScannerScreenTest` confirm-surface cases (verbatim fp, content-desc, Confirm/Decline click, 48dp targets). Depends on #320 (parse) + #342 (derivation), both merged; split from #321.
- [Paste code dialog](features/paste-code-dialog.md) — the manual paste-code pairing fallback for when the QR camera can't scan (dialog shipped inline in #503, extracted + re-routed through the gate in #501); stateless M3 `AlertDialog` `PasteCodeDialog(onDismiss, onValidPayload: (String)->Unit)` in `ui/onboarding/`. **Store-free by design (#501)**: its only success output is the raw validated pairing string handed to `onValidPayload` — it holds NO reference to `PairedServerStore`/`confirmPairAndNavigate` and NEVER persists, so "paste cannot persist" is a structural TYPE guarantee (the belt-and-suspenders split: structural fact + a UI test pinning the callback contract, not two stochastic checks). The `Pair` button parses the **trimmed** `pasteText` ONCE via `parsePairingPayload` and hands *that same* string off on `Success` (no suspension between parse and callback → parsed-from == emitted, so the fingerprint the user later confirms derives from this exact string); `Failure` sets the inline `"Invalid pairing code"` and emits NOTHING (a bad paste never reaches the QR-worded full-screen `PARSE_FAILED_MSG`, AC#4). `MainActivity` feeds the payload to the same `ScannerEvent.QrDecoded` the camera uses → `Decoded` → the shared `LaunchedEffect` re-parses + derives `serverKeyFingerprint` (#342) → `AwaitingConfirm` → persist ONLY behind Confirm (the [Pairing confirm gate](features/pairing-confirm-gate.md), #343) — closing the impostor-server MITM gap on the paste entry path. Owns `pasteText`/`pasteError` via `remember` (**not** `rememberSaveable`; mounts fresh per show so state re-inits, retiring #503's explicit reset). Logs NOTHING (MUST-hold — the token must never reach `Log.*`; the redacting `toString()` on `QrDecoded`/`Decoded`, `<redacted N chars>`, covers the downstream). 4 instrumented contract tests (`PasteCodeDialogTest`: render / valid-hands-off-no-error / whitespace-trim / invalid-inline-error-no-handoff / cancel; valid-code fixture built inline with `java.util.Base64` since the `src/test/` `wrap(json())` helper isn't on the androidTest classpath — compiled not run, no emulator). Second producer of `QrDecoded` — deliberately NOT renamed source-neutral (rename fan-out across the VM+test+camera > XS).
- [Data model](features/data-model.md) — `Conversation` / `Session` / `Message` schema in `data/model/`; shared shape across mobile, Discord, and pyrycode CLI consumers. Sentinel `DefaultScratchCwd` co-lives in `Conversation.kt` as the single-source-of-truth marker for unbound-workspace conversations. `Conversation.isSleeping: Boolean = false` (#20) is derived in Phase 1 and wire-parsed in Phase 4. `Conversation.archived: Boolean = false` (#93) is the authoritative archive bit — flipped by `archive(id)`, parsed from the wire in Phase 4. Since #191, `Message` carries a `toolCall: ToolCall? = null` (new top-level `data class ToolCall(toolName, input, output)` co-located in `Message.kt`) — the structured payload `Role.Tool` messages need so #131's `ToolCallRow` can discriminate tool name / input / output without parsing free-form strings; field is last + defaulted so every positional and named `Message(...)` call site is source-compatible. The invariant `toolCall != null iff role == Role.Tool` is documented via KDoc on the field, not enforced at construction (Phase 0's only producer is `FakeConversationRepository`; runtime enforcement would defend an unobserved failure mode). Sealed `Message` hierarchy was deliberately not chosen for size discipline; deferred promotion costs nothing extra if #131 finds the lack of type-level discrimination painful.
- [Conversation repository](features/conversation-repository.md) — `ConversationRepository` interface + `ConversationFilter` (`All` / `Channels` / `Discussions` / `Archived` since #93), `ThreadItem` (`MessageItem` / `SessionBoundary`), `BoundaryReason`; data-layer seam every UI tier binds to. Phase 1 binding is the in-memory `FakeConversationRepository`; the `observeConversations` projection also stamps the derived `Conversation.isSleeping` flag (#20). Filter matrix (#93): `All` returns everything including archived; `Channels` / `Discussions` carry an explicit `!archived` clause; `Archived` returns archived rows regardless of `isPromoted`. `archive(id)` since #93 flips the flag instead of removing the entry; `unarchive(id)` since #96 is the symmetric inverse (silent-idempotent flag flip back to `false`, same `unknown(id)` throw on missing). Since #161 a sibling `observeLastMessage(id): Flow<Message?>` projects the most-recent `Message` by `Message.timestamp` from the same state-holder (`state.map { records[id]?.messages?.maxByOrNull { it.timestamp } }`); unknown id and known-with-no-messages both collapse to `null`. `seedDiscussion(...)` gained an optional `messages: List<SeedMessage> = emptyList()` parameter mirroring `seedChannel`'s `currentMessages` so `seed-discussion-a` ships with 4 alternating `User`/`Assistant` messages anchored on `lastUsedAt = 2026-05-11T14:00:00Z` (seed invariant: `seed.lastUsedAt == seed.messages.last().timestamp`); `seed-discussion-b` and `seed-discussion-archived` stay empty so the null path is also reachable. Since #187 `sendMessage(id, text): Message` appends a user-authored `Message(role = Role.User, sessionId = currentSessionId, timestamp = now, isStreaming = false, id = UUID.randomUUID().toString())` to the conversation's `messages` and bumps `Conversation.lastUsedAt` to the same `now` (captured once at the top of the mutator so observers downstream of `observeLastMessage` + `observeConversations` see the same `Instant` by construction); follows the `rename` / `mintNewSession` `lateinit var`-escape template, throws `IllegalArgumentException` via `unknown(id)` on missing record, does **not** trim or reject blank `text` (UI-layer concern). The interface widening cascaded a mechanical `TODO("not used")` override into 10 anonymous `object : ConversationRepository { … }` stubs across 4 test files (`SettingsViewModelTest`, `ChannelListViewModelTest`, `DiscussionListViewModelTest`, `ArchivedDiscussionsViewModelTest`) — no VM under test consumes `sendMessage` yet (lands in the follow-up thread input bar ticket split from #133). Since #191, `SeedMessage`/`seedMsg(...)` gained an optional `toolCall: ToolCall? = null` field/param (same defaulted-passthrough shape as `isStreaming` from #184) that `seedChannel`'s historical + current loops and `seedDiscussion`'s message loop propagate into the constructed `Message`; one realistic `Role.Tool` message (`toolName = "Read"`, `input` = a `ThreadScreen.kt` path, `output` = a small `@Composable` excerpt) is seeded into `seed-channel-pyrycode-mobile`'s current session at `2026-05-10T08:59:00Z` (between the existing 08:58 user message and the streaming 09:00 assistant — preserves the single derived `SessionBoundary` and chronology). One tool message is sufficient for AC; tool messages in other channels / historical sessions / multiples are #131's call when its render fixture needs them. Since #192, `ThreadItem.SessionBoundary` carries an optional `workspaceCwd: String? = null` (non-null iff `reason == WorkspaceChange`; invariant documented in KDoc, asserted in tests, not `require(...)`-enforced — same posture as `Message.toolCall` (#191), `Conversation.archived` (#93), `Conversation.isSleeping` (#20)), and `SeedSession` gains paired `nextBoundaryReason: BoundaryReason? = null` / `nextWorkspaceCwd: String? = null` fields describing the boundary that follows that historical session. `seedChannel` flattens these into a per-record `boundariesBySessionId: Map<String, AuthoredBoundary>` keyed by **successor session id** via `mapIndexedNotNull` over `orderedSessionIds = history.map { it.sessionId } + sessionId` (current session id is the tail anchor); `buildThreadItems` now takes the map alongside `messages`, looks up `boundariesBySessionId[message.sessionId]` on each session-id delta, and either uses the authored `reason`/`workspaceCwd` or falls back to `BoundaryReason.Clear` + `null` (the unchanged default — preserves the `initialMessages` constructor path used by `observeMessages_messagesAcrossTwoSessions_emitsExactlyOneBoundary` plus any future mutator-minted sessions). The three seeded channels collectively exercise all three `BoundaryReason` variants, one per channel: `seed-channel-personal` → `Clear` (no path; `/clear` story), `seed-channel-pyrycode-mobile` → `WorkspaceChange` with `"~/Workspace/pyrycode-mobile"` (path matches the channel's own `cwd` — "user switched into this workspace to start the next session"), `seed-channel-joi-pilates` → `IdleEvict` (no path; re-uses the channel's existing `currentSessionEndedAt = 2026-05-09T03:00:00Z` sleeping-indicator posture). Map-by-successor-session-id is forward-compatible with the deferred mutator-side eager-emission follow-up (`mintNewSession(reason, workspaceCwd)` writes `boundariesBySessionId[newSessionId] = AuthoredBoundary(...)` inside the existing `state.update { ... }` block; no storage reshape needed). `AuthoredBoundary(reason, workspaceCwd)` is a private supporting data class next to `ConversationRecord` — repository-private, not in `data/model/`, keeps the cross-package model portable per CLAUDE.md "Don't" rules. `ConversationRepository` interface untouched; `Session` / `Conversation` models untouched. Four new tests in `FakeConversationRepositoryTest`: three per-channel scenarios + an aggregate guard (`seededChannels_collectivelyExerciseAllBoundaryReasons`) asserting the set of reasons across the three channels equals `{Clear, IdleEvict, WorkspaceChange}` — specifically catches the silent-regression failure mode where a future seed edit collapses two channels onto the same variant. Since #209 the interface also carries `recentWorkspaces(): Flow<List<String>>` (deduped, most-recent-first, excludes `""` and `DEFAULT_SCRATCH_CWD`) backing the Workspace Picker's Recent section — first member on the interface to ship with a `flowOf(emptyList())` default body (deliberate cascade-escape: 11 anonymous `object : ConversationRepository` fakes across 5 test files inherit untouched; default is semantically meaningful — "a repo that doesn't track workspace history has no recents" — not a placeholder). `FakeConversationRepository` adds a sibling `private val recents = MutableStateFlow<List<String>>(initialRecents())` next to `state`; `initialRecents()` is a companion-object projection over `SEED_RECORDS` (`sortedByDescending { lastUsedAt }.map { cwd }.filterNot { isEmpty() || == DEFAULT_SCRATCH_CWD }.distinct()` → `["~/Workspace/pyrycode-mobile", "~/Workspace/joi-pilates", "~/Workspace/personal"]` at class load). Bumped via `private fun bumpWorkspace(cwd)` (`if (cwd.isEmpty() || cwd == DEFAULT_SCRATCH_CWD) return; recents.update { listOf(cwd) + it.filterNot { it == cwd } }`) called from `createDiscussion` / `promote` / `mintNewSession` (covers both `changeWorkspace` and `startNewSession`) after each `state.update { }` block; `rename` / `sendMessage` / `archive` / `unarchive` don't touch cwd and don't bump. Filter-at-insert keeps the sentinel pair off the list entirely so the read path is filter-free. Two atomic writes per mutator (`state` then `recents`) acceptable — no cross-flow invariant. `lateinit var resultCwd` escapes `mintNewSession`'s post-update cwd out of the `state.update` lambda (same shape as #187's `lateinit var newSession`). Three new tests in `FakeConversationRepositoryTest` (`recentWorkspaces_dedupes_repeatedCwds`, `recentWorkspaces_ordersByMostRecentWrite`, `recentWorkspaces_excludesEmptyStringAndDefaultScratch`); zero edits to the 5 test files holding anonymous fakes (`SettingsViewModelTest`, `ArchivedDiscussionsViewModelTest`, `DiscussionListViewModelTest`, `ChannelListViewModelTest`, `ThreadViewModelTest`) — the default body absorbs the cascade. Since #210 the interface also carries the write-side counterpart `createWorkspaceFolder(name: String): String` (creates a folder under the literal `pyry-workspace/` prefix, validates `require(name.isNotBlank())` for `IllegalArgumentException` on `""`/`"  "`/`"\t\n"`, returns the constructed path, and bumps it to index 0 of `recents` via the same `bumpWorkspace` helper landed in #209) — second member on the interface to ship with a default body, this one `error("createWorkspaceFolder is not implemented for this ConversationRepository")` (no semantically-meaningful zero for a path `String` — the throw is the right shape; `error(...)` over `NotImplementedError` for the clearer call-site message). Production consumer is the Workspace Picker's **Other → Create new folder…** dialog (Figma `19:44`); test fakes never invoke it so the default is unreachable in tests today. Fake override is 5 lines (no new state, no new helpers, no `state.update {}` — there's no `Conversation` to mutate, just the projection); two new tests in `FakeConversationRepositoryTest` (`createWorkspaceFolder_appearsAtPositionZeroOfRecents`, `createWorkspaceFolder_blankName_throwsIllegalArgumentException`); zero edits to the 5 test files holding anonymous fakes — the default body absorbs the cascade exactly as #209's did. Since #218 the interface also carries `delete(conversationId: String): Unit` — destructive counterpart to `archive` / `unarchive` (no `undelete`; the soft reversible action remains `archive`) and third member on the interface to ship with a default body (`error("delete is not implemented for this ConversationRepository")`, same cascade-escape lever absorbing 11 anonymous fakes across 5 test files). Default is `error(...)` rather than a silent `Unit` no-op even though `Unit` makes "no-op" syntactically free — silent success in a default body would let tests pass vacuously on under-stubbed fakes wired to expect deletion to do something. **Deliberate deviation from the failures-throw convention:** unknown `conversationId` is a silent no-op (not `IllegalArgumentException` like every other mutator). The contract converges on the post-condition — after a successful return, the conversation is absent from `observeConversations` — and both an unknown id and a successful delete share that post-condition, so distinguishing them buys nothing for the caller. Deviation is documented in the interface KDoc with an explicit contrast line against `archive` / `unarchive`. Fake override is one line (`override suspend fun delete(conversationId: String) { state.update { it - conversationId } }`) — `Map - missingKey` returns an `equals`-identical map → `MutableStateFlow.update` does not re-emit, giving free idempotency from the same mechanism `archive`'s `record.copy(... archived = true)` uses. No `bumpWorkspace` (delete removes the record; recents are unaffected — same as `archive` / `unarchive`), no `lastUsedAt` bump (lifecycle transition, not usage). Stream convergence is automatic and the architect-resolved decision is **re-emit empty, do not complete**: `observeMessages(deletedId)` → `emptyList()` via the existing `records[id] ?: return@map emptyList()` fallback, `observeLastMessage(deletedId)` → `null` via `records[id]?.messages?.maxByOrNull { … }`. Completing the flow on delete would silently lose long-lived collector subscriptions (e.g. a `StateFlow`-backed `UiState` projection in a still-composed ViewModel during a race) forever; re-emit-empty preserves the subscription. Five new tests in `FakeConversationRepositoryTest` (`delete_removesConversation_from_observeConversations_All`, `delete_onUnknownId_doesNotThrow` — bare-bodied, no try/catch, "no exception escapes" is the assertion, `delete_isIdempotent`, `delete_causes_observeMessages_toReEmitEmpty` + `…_toReEmitNull` — both reuse `seed-channel-pyrycode-mobile` to anchor the architect-resolved re-emit-empty decision in the test suite); zero edits to the 5 test files holding anonymous fakes. Production consumer is the Channel Info sheet's **Delete** action (host ticket deferred from #144); this slice is data-layer-only, no UI touched. Since #267 the file also carries the idle-archive policy `Conversation.lastUsedAt` was missing: a top-level `internal val ARCHIVE_IDLE_THRESHOLD: Duration = 30.days` (a `val`, not `const` — `Duration` isn't const-able), a pure top-level `internal fun shouldArchive(conversation, referenceTime): Boolean` (`!isPromoted && referenceTime - lastUsedAt >= ARCHIVE_IDLE_THRESHOLD` — promoted channels never archive, **inclusive** `>=` boundary, caller-supplied `referenceTime` so the rule never reads the system clock → deterministic boundary tests with literal `Instant`s, no fake clock), and a public `suspend fun sweep(referenceTime): Int` **on the fake only, deliberately not on the interface** (the Phase 4 backend sweeps server-side on its own ticker; off-interface means the anonymous test stubs need zero changes — additive, no consumer cascade). `sweep` applies `shouldArchive` across the store in one `state.update { records -> records.mapValues { … } }`, flipping `archived = true` on each unpromoted, not-already-archived match and returning the count **newly** archived — converging on the same `archived` flag manual `archive()` flips (swept discussions leave Channels/Discussions, appear under Archived + All, stay `unarchive()`-able; no parallel hidden path). The newly-archived counter is **reset to 0 at the top of the `update` lambda** because `MutableStateFlow.update` runs in a compare-and-set retry loop and may re-invoke the lambda under contention — initialising once outside would double-count. Idempotent + no spurious re-emit: a second sweep at the same reference time returns 0 and the rebuilt map is value-equal so `StateFlow` skips the emission. **No construction-time sweep and no read-time derivation:** a construction-time `sweep(Clock.System.now())` would be a time-bomb auto-archiving the fixed May-2026 seeds once wall-clock crosses +30 days (breaking the `Discussions=2`/`Archived=1` seed-count tests on a calendar date with no code change), and a read-time derivation would defeat `unarchive()` (next read re-derives an idle discussion as archived). The live trigger (a scheduler / lifecycle hook calling `sweep(Clock.System.now())`) is deferred to Phase 4 with the backend — predicate + sweep primitive are complete and test-exercised now, not dead code. Kotlin port of the canonical pyrycode (Go) `ShouldArchive(c, now)`; uses only `kotlin.time` + `kotlinx.datetime`, no new dependency. Four new tests in `FakeConversationRepositoryTest` (boundary matrix via a `conv(promoted, lastUsedAt)` helper; sweep integration `n==2` + convergence on Archived/All + channels untouched + nothing deleted; idempotence; restorability via `unarchive`); no UI change — no screen consumes `sweep` yet. See `../codebase/267.md`.
- [Remote conversation repository](features/remote-conversation-repository.md) — the Phase 4 `ConversationRepository` implementation (`data/repository/RemoteConversationRepository.kt`), the live counterpart to the in-memory [Fake](features/conversation-repository.md). #312 wires the **conversation-list read path** (`observeConversations`) live over the [Noise session pump](features/noise-session-pump.md) (#309) consumed through a new consumer-defined `SessionPump` interface (`inbound: Flow<Envelope>` + `send(Envelope): Boolean`, the [`ConnectionStateSource`](features/connection-state.md) precedent; `NoiseSessionPump : SessionPump` + the live Koin binding are #279/#302's, not #312's). A single `init`-launched collector is the **sole** consumer of the hot single-consumer `pump.inbound` and demultiplexes by `Envelope.type` into one `MutableStateFlow<List<Conversation>?>` projection; the cold `observeConversations(filter)` fans out from it (issues `list_conversations` with a `{}` payload on every subscribe — redundant requests absorbed by `StateFlow` conflation — then `project()`s the latest snapshot: `filter` per `ConversationFilter` then `sortedByDescending { lastUsedAt }`, **identical to the Fake** so the UI is binding-agnostic). Any inbound `conversations` snapshot (request reply **or** unsolicited change push) is the list source of truth, decoded+mapped via the [#316](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) `ConversationsPayload.toConversations()` boundary — no `in_reply_to` correlation needed for re-emit; a malformed payload is dropped in one `catch (IllegalArgumentException)` (covers both #316 decode families: `MissingFieldException`⊂`SerializationException` + the kotlinx-datetime bad-timestamp throw) so the lone collector survives. **[#329](codebase/329.md) landed** the `observeLastMessage` last-message preview live: the `conversations` wire carries no message content (only `last_message_ts`, mapping to no domain field), so the preview rides the **live `message` stream** — a `message` branch in `onInbound` maps each payload via the [#317](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) `MessagePayloadDto.toMessage` boundary (`sessionId=""`, a list-tier placeholder the preview never reads), keys by the DTO's `conversation_id` (the domain `Message` carries none), and folds the strictly-greater-by-`timestamp` message into a second `lastMessages: MutableStateFlow<Map<String, Message>>` via `MutableStateFlow.update { }`; `observeLastMessage` is a cold `lastMessages.map { it[id] }.distinctUntilChanged()` projection (no `pump.send`, no new wire type), `null` on unknown/empty; the preview's cold-start still rides live `message`s only (#313 feeds backfill into the thread projection, not `lastMessages`). **[#313](codebase/313.md) landed** the `observeMessages` thread read — backfilled history merged **ahead of** the live `message` stream into one chronological, `message_id`-deduped `List<ThreadItem.MessageItem>` in **wire/arrival order** (a `message_chunk` carries one envelope `ts` for many rows, so **no client-side timestamp sort**): a third `messagesByConversation: MutableStateFlow<Map<String, List<Message>>>` projection fed by the same `init` collector from **two** demux arms — a new `message_chunk` arm (the `backfill_since` response body; each row self-routes by its own `conversation_id`, mapped via #317's `toMessage`, the whole chunk decoded under one try/catch so a single bad row drops the batch) and the existing `message` arm now **also** appending the live row — accumulated by `appendMessages` (first-seen `message_id` appends, a repeat id replaces in place at its first position, last write wins); `observeMessages` is a cold `flow { pump.send(backfillSinceRequest(id)); emitAll(messagesByConversation → `MessageItem`s with `distinctUntilChanged`) }`, re-issuing `backfill_since` per subscribe (re-delivery absorbed by dedup, pre-`Open` send self-heals). Wire SSOT #272 `BackfillSincePayload` = `{since_ts, conversation_id, max_messages}` (epoch `since_ts` = all history + advisory `max_messages` cap) — the spec's guessed `{conversation_id, since}` corrected; `backfill_done` is an informational no-op. `MessageItem`s only at #313; **[#336](codebase/336.md) landed** the session-boundary fold once the upstream gate closed (pyrycode#656/#657/#740/#741/#739): the store was **unified `messagesByConversation` → `threadByConversation: MutableStateFlow<Map<String, List<ThreadItem>>>`** (the five `Message.id`-keyed folds lifted to `ThreadItem` output-preserving via a shared `is ThreadItem.MessageItem` guard — `appendMessages` keeps role-agnostic id-only dedup), and a capability-gated `session_transition` event decodes (`SessionTransitionPayloadDto.toBoundary()`, unknown `reason` → null drop, `workspace_cwd` the one nullable field) and **pure-appends** a `ThreadItem.SessionBoundary` keyed strictly by payload `conversation_id` (no dedup, no cross-route, no `liveSessionEvents`/stall touch, silent drop — **live transitions only**, backfill carries none) → see [Session-transition fold](features/session-transition-fold.md). Live token-streaming rides the separate structured `assistant_delta` stream (#385/#337), not the finished-only `message` path. **[#346](codebase/346.md)** filled `sendMessage` live — the **first mutation** — and added the shared `ack`/`error` request↔reply correlation primitive (`pendingRequests` registry + `sendAndAwaitReply`; keyed on the request *envelope* id, not the payload `message_id`): mint a client `message_id`, send `send_message` `{conversation_id, message_id, text}`, await the correlated empty `ack` (success) or `error` (failure — `conversation.not_found`→`IllegalArgumentException` fake-parity, else `RelayErrorException(code, retryable, message)`; not-`Open` `send`→false→`IllegalStateException`), then **confirmed-insert** a reconstructed `Role.User` `Message` (`sessionId=""`) into **both** `lastMessages` + `messagesByConversation` only after the ack (PO `security-sensitive` decision: confirmed-insert NOT optimistic — the thread never shows a message the server did not receive, by construction; **no server echo to the sender** — corrects the false "map the send_message echo via #317" premise, [[phase4-send-message-acks-not-message-echo]]). **[#347](codebase/347.md)** filled `createDiscussion` live — the **second** mutation — composing #346's `sendAndAwaitReply` + #318's `ConversationResponseDto.toConversation()` with a new encode-only `create_conversation` request DTO (`CreateConversationPayloadDto`: `is_promoted=false` + optional `cwd`; `name` unmodeled — discussions are server-auto-named; a null `cwd` is omitted under `explicitNulls=false` → `{"is_promoted":false}`, wire-compatible with the server's `*string` no-`omitempty` field = server picks the scratch cwd): send `create_conversation`, await the correlated **typed** `conversation_created` reply (routed through the **same** `ack` success arm as one shared branch, decoded in the **caller's** coroutine so a malformed reply never throws inside the lone collector), then **confirmed-insert** the decoded `Conversation` into the list `projection` via a new `upsertConversation` CAS upsert-by-id (the projection's second writer; runs only after the decode succeeds, so no failure path corrupts the list — AC #3) so `observeConversations` re-emits with it (AC #2); the returned `cwd` is the **server's** reply value, never the input (AC #1). **[#348](codebase/348.md)** filled `promote` live — the **third and last** #314 mutation — reusing #346's `sendAndAwaitReply`/`mapError` + #347's `upsertConversation` fold verbatim and adding one new encode-only `PromoteConversationPayloadDto` (`promote_conversation` request — all three fields `conversation_id`/`name`/`cwd` **required/non-null**, contrast #347's optional `cwd`): a null `workspace` ("promote in place") resolves to the conversation's existing `cwd` from the `projection.value` snapshot (the remote analog of the fake's `workspace ?: record.cwd`; `?: ""` unreachable-from-UI fallback, tested, no throw added), send `promote_conversation`, await the typed `conversation_updated` reply (routed through the **same** success arm as `conversation_created`, decoded via #318's `ConversationResponseDto` — the one DTO modelling both — in the caller's coroutine), then **confirmed-upsert** the promoted `Conversation` so `observeConversations` flips it into the Channels tier and out of Discussions (AC #2, in-place, no duplicate); the returned `cwd`/`name`/`isPromoted` are the **server's** reply values (AC #1), and `conversation.not_found`→`IllegalArgumentException` is exercised here for the first time (unlike create, promote references an existing conversation; `mapError` reused unchanged). **[#530](codebase/530.md)** filled `rename` live — the **fourth** mutation — reusing #346's `sendAndAwaitReply`/`mapError` + #347's `upsertConversation` fold verbatim and adding one new encode-only `RenameConversationPayloadDto` (`rename_conversation` request — `conversation_id`/`name` only, deliberately NOT `promote`'s DTO since a rename has no `cwd`): send `rename_conversation`, await the typed `conversation_updated` reply (same success arm #348 wired for `promote`, decoded via #318's `ConversationResponseDto`), then **confirmed-upsert** the renamed `Conversation` so `observeConversations` and the thread top bar `displayName` both re-emit from one fold; the returned `name` is the **server's** reply value. **[#539](codebase/539.md)** filled `startNewSession` live — the **fifth** mutation, and the first that is **fire-and-forget rather than request-reply**: the daemon's `new_session` v2 control frame (pyrycode#831) carries no payload and owes no ack/reply/broadcast, so this is a line-for-line mirror of `interrupt()` one verb over — `check(pump.send(newSessionFrame())) { ISE }` via plain `SessionPump.send`, never `sendAndAwaitReply` (would hang). Success (the new session's real identity) is observed later via the pre-existing `session_transition` marker the #336 fold already renders — out of scope for this send-only slice; the method returns a locally-constructed placeholder `Session` with empty-string identity fields (`id`/`claudeSessionUuid`) since the interface forces a `Session` return but the wire yields none, and the sole consumer ([#540](codebase/540.md), landed) discards it. `mutationsSupported` stays `false` (at the time, its siblings `archive`/`unarchive`/`changeWorkspace` still threw; all three are now wired — see below). `recentWorkspaces`/`createWorkspaceFolder` keep their interface defaults (not overridden, no stub). Siblings extend the **same class + `onInbound` `when`** (no second inbound collector); single-row `conversation_updated`/`conversation_created` deltas (#318) are no-ops here, owned by #347/#348 — hence #312 depends on #316 only, not #318. Zero `android.*`, dispatcher inherited from the injected connection scope. **[#543](codebase/543.md)** filled `setSessionSettings` live — the **sixth** mutation, and the **first session-scoped** one (keyed by `sessionId`, not `conversationId`): a direct analogue of `rename` ([#530](codebase/530.md)) — encode → `sendAndAwaitReply` → typed-decode — **minus the state fold**, since the daemon's `session_settings_updated` ack echoes only the input `session_id` (session settings live in the ViewModel, #544, not this repo's projection). `SetSessionSettingsPayloadDto` carries `model`/`effort`/`yolo` as independently-nullable fields under `explicitNulls = false` — a **presence contract** mirroring the server struct's pointer + `omitempty` fields (`null` = "leave unchanged" and is omitted from the wire; a non-null value, including `false`/`""`, is always sent), so a single-control change sends only that one field. Unlike `rename`/`promote` the reply is a **new** verb (`session_settings_updated`, not the reused `conversation_updated`), added to the same reply-completion demux arm; its unhosted-session error code is `session.not_found` (not `conversation.not_found`) so it maps to `RelayErrorException`, never `IllegalArgumentException` — no IAE-crash path, unlike every conversation-scoped mutation. Data-layer slice of the #536 split ([#543](codebase/543.md) data → #544 ViewModel/facade → #545 e2e); the `StableConversationRepository` facade delegation is deliberately deferred to #544, its first caller. **[#549](codebase/549.md)** filled `archive`/`unarchive` live — the **sixth and seventh** mutations, both delegating to one private `sendArchiveToggle(conversationId, type)` helper parameterized by the wire-type string (`archive_conversation`/`unarchive_conversation`, server pyrycode#881's shared payload for the pair), reusing `sendAndAwaitReply`/`mapError`/the existing `conversation_updated` demux arm verbatim; the load-bearing change is extending the shared `ConversationResponseDto` with a **defaulted** `is_archived` field (absent → `false`, present → real value) wired into `toConversation()` (was hardcoded `archived = false`), which as a side effect also fixes a dormant bug where the already-shipped `rename` was silently dropping the same field. `mutationsSupported` stays `false` (untouched; at the time `changeWorkspace` was the only remaining throw — it was wired next, by [#560](codebase/560.md)). Data-layer slice of the #531 split (#549 data → #550 ViewModel surfacing → #551 e2e); the list-read path's own `is_archived` gap (`ConversationSummaryDto`) is a separate, unfiled follow-up. **[#359](codebase/359.md)** added `registerPushToken(token)` — the **first method that is NOT a `ConversationRepository` interface override** (push-token registration is a device/connection concern, so it lives on the concrete repo only and deliberately does **not** flow through the [`StableConversationRepository`](features/stable-conversation-repository.md) facade ViewModels hold; reaching it from a live caller is the Firebase sibling's job). Encodes a `register_push_token` request (`{platform:"fcm", token, device_name}`), reuses #346's `sendAndAwaitReply` + `mapError`→`RelayErrorException` **verbatim** (no new `onInbound` branch — `ack`/`error` already wired; no new error mapping; `server.binary_busy` retryable / `auth.invalid_token` not, so a caller branches on `retryable`), and — uniquely among the mutations — **mutates no projection** (registers a token, produces no domain object; success = the call returned without throwing). `device_name` is a **last/defaulted** ctor param (zero edit fan-out; `AppModule`+coordinator untouched, so off the now-merged #352 conflict path); the default `""` defer was **closed by [#365](codebase/365.md)** — its connect-time hook is the **first live caller** and `AppModule` now threads the live `NoiseClientInfo.deviceName`, so `""` is no longer the production value; the capability stays **dormant** until Firebase #361 stores a token. See [`codebase/312.md`](codebase/312.md) (list path) + [`codebase/329.md`](codebase/329.md) (last-message) + [`codebase/313.md`](codebase/313.md) (thread + backfill) + [`codebase/359.md`](codebase/359.md) (push registration) + [`codebase/365.md`](codebase/365.md) (connect-time re-registration) + [`codebase/543.md`](codebase/543.md) (session settings, first session-scoped mutation). **[#532](codebase/532.md)** filled `delete` live — the **eighth** mutation and the **first REMOVE-shaped** one: unlike `rename`/`archive`/`unarchive` (which all reuse the pre-existing `conversation_updated` demux arm), delete's reply is a genuinely new correlated type (`conversation_deleted`, bare `{id}` ack), so `onInbound`'s success `when` arm required a real registration (`TYPE_CONVERSATION_DELETED`) — skipping it would leave the ack unrouted and `sendAndAwaitReply` would hang until teardown. The fold is a new `removeConversation` helper (the contrast to `upsertConversation`) that filters the id out of **all three** read projections (`projection`, `threadByConversation`, `lastMessages`), matching the fake's unified-record removal the remote's three separate `StateFlow`s otherwise can't mirror in one write. `conversation.not_found` **converges as success** (removes locally, returns normally) rather than propagating `IllegalArgumentException` like `rename`/`archive` — the interface's delete contract is explicitly tolerant of unknown ids, the deliberate divergence from the family's IAE-crash-on-not-found posture the [#490](codebase/490.md) guard relies on. `mutationsSupported` stays `false`; the rung-3 e2e is #554 (Inbox, blocked by this ticket). See [`codebase/532.md`](codebase/532.md). **[#560](codebase/560.md)** filled `changeWorkspace` live — the **ninth and last** mutation, closing out every `UnsupportedOperationException` stub on this class: a `rename`-shaped body (send `change_workspace` `{conversation_id, cwd}`, reused `conversation_updated` reply via #318's `ConversationResponseDto`, `upsertConversation` fold — server-authoritative `cwd`, not the request's), plus the new encode-only `ChangeWorkspacePayloadDto` and a `TYPE_CHANGE_WORKSPACE` constant; **no demux change** (`conversation_updated` already routed since #348/#530, unlike delete's genuinely-new reply type). Signature stays `: Session` — the reply carries no session identity and `change_workspace` performs **no session transition** (pyrycode#823 Out-of-Scope), so the method returns the same vestigial placeholder `Session` shape `startNewSession` ([#539](codebase/539.md)) established, avoiding a 22-site interface/fake/facade/test-double fan-out. `conversation.not_found`→`IllegalArgumentException` (uncaught by the [#490](codebase/490.md) guard, family-consistent with `rename`); every other server error (e.g. an out-of-`$HOME` path, `protocol.malformed`)→`RelayErrorException`; not-connected→`IllegalStateException`. `mutationsSupported` stays `false`, untouched. `FakeConversationRepository.changeWorkspace` is deliberately left mismatched (mints a session transition the real daemon doesn't perform) — an evidence-based divergence, not a bug. Surfacing is [#561](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/issues/561) (blocked by this ticket); the rung-3 e2e is [#562](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/issues/562) (Inbox, family-gated by #537). See [`codebase/560.md`](codebase/560.md).
- [Dependency injection](features/dependency-injection.md) — Koin scaffold; two modules at `di/AppModule.kt` loaded by `startKoin` in `PyryApp.onCreate`: `appModule` (every `single`/`viewModel`) + the #350 `conversationRepositoryModule(useRelay = BuildConfig.USE_RELAY_REPOSITORY)` selector — the **only** module that binds `ConversationRepository`, flag-selecting `FakeConversationRepository` (default OFF) or the relay-backed [`StableConversationRepository`](features/stable-conversation-repository.md) facade ([#350](codebase/350.md)); `appModule` registers both candidates concrete-only. Downstream tickets append `single`/`viewModel` lines; a build-flag-selected binding goes in its own module per the #350 pattern.
- [Connection state](features/connection-state.md) — `data/model/ConnectionState.kt` (#196) is a sealed class with four cases — `Connected` / `Connecting` / `Reconnecting(secondsRemaining: Int)` / `Offline` — fixed by product copy for the [`ConnectionBanner`](features/connection-banner.md) (UI slice #200, split from #197) and the follow-up wiring slice that places the banner inside `ThreadScreen`. `Reconnecting.secondsRemaining` is `Int` (not `kotlin.time.Duration`) because the banner interpolates it into the literal `"Reconnecting in Ns"`; no `require(...)` on the field per the data-model "plain DTO, no constructor invariants" posture shared with `Conversation.archived` (#93), `Message.toolCall` (#191), and `SessionBoundary.workspaceCwd` (#192). Behind the model: `data/repository/ConnectionStateSource` interface with `fun observe(): Flow<ConnectionState>` + `suspend fun retry()` — the load-bearing contract that #197's `ThreadViewModel` consumes; Phase 4 swaps only the Koin binding. Phase 2 fake `FakeConnectionStateSource` wraps a single `MutableStateFlow<ConnectionState>` initialised to `Connected`, exposes `observe()` via `state.asStateFlow()` widened to `Flow<ConnectionState>` so the interface doesn't leak `StateFlow` (Phase 4 can pick a cold `flow { … }` shape), and carries an empty `retry()` body with a `// Phase 2: no-op stub` comment marking the deliberate emptiness. Test/preview seam is a **public `fun emit(state: ConnectionState)` on the concrete fake, deliberately not on the interface** — production consumers resolve `ConnectionStateSource` via Koin and cannot reach `emit()` by accident; the public mutator on a fake but absent from its interface is the established pattern for "drive state from a test/preview without leaking the affordance to production code". Koin binding `single { FakeConnectionStateSource() } bind ConnectionStateSource::class` added immediately after the `FakeConversationRepository` line in `AppModule.kt:31` (singleton scope — Phase 4 will hold a long-lived WebSocket, binding shape stays the same). Unit-tested by three JUnit4 methods (`observe_emits_Connected_by_default`, `emit_drivesAllFourStates_inOrder`, `retry_is_callable_without_throwing`) in the project's plain `runBlocking` + `Flow.first()` idiom — **per-state `.first()` after each `emit(...)`** instead of the spec's "collect into list" plan because `MutableStateFlow` conflates intermediate emissions across a single subscription (an inline comment in the test pins the reason); the AC's "surfaces each in order via `observe()`" is satisfied by the per-state shape. Lives in `data/repository/` (not a new `data/connection/` package) because the existing convention is "repository" = any cold-flow + suspend-mutator data source. **Phase 4 walk-back landed in [#307](codebase/307.md)** — the real source is the OkHttp-WS-backed [`RelayConnectionSupervisor`](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) under `data/network/` (**not** Ktor as this entry originally guessed): the Koin binding was swapped to it, `connect()`/`close()` exposed for #302, and `FakeConnectionStateSource` was **kept** (only its binding removed — still used by `ThreadViewModelTest`/`FakeConnectionStateSourceTest`). The interface did not change. **Since [#391](codebase/391.md) `ConnectionState` is derived, not the supervisor's source of truth** — the relay-leg [`RelayLinkStatus`](features/relay-link-status.md) (these four cases + `DaemonAbsent`) is, and `observe()` maps it back per-collector (`DaemonAbsent → Offline`); this model, the interface, and the four product-copy cases are unchanged (Strangler Fig — `DaemonAbsent` was deliberately **not** added here, which would break the banner's exhaustive `when`).
- [Mobile Protocol v2 wire layer](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) — root of the Phase 4 wire layer (#273): five `@Serializable` models in `data/network/MobileWireModels.kt` (`InnerFrameV2{v=2,type,data}`, `Envelope{id:Long,type,ts:String,payload:JsonElement,in_reply_to?}`, `HelloClientPayload`, `HelloAckPayload`, `QrPayload`) for the exact byte contract the pyrycode v2 server speaks (proven by the 2026-05-29 Noise spike) + a thin top-level codec in `MobileWireCodec.kt`. Wire is snake_case ↔ camelCase via `@SerialName` — **load-bearing for Go interop, not cosmetic**. `Envelope.payload` is a generic `JsonElement` carrier polymorphic by `type` (no sealed payload zoo; consumers bridge via `decodeFromJsonElement`/`encodeToJsonElement`); only the two Hello payloads are typed this phase. The codec is the configured `val MobileJson: Json { encodeDefaults = true; explicitNulls = false; ignoreUnknownKeys = true }` (each flag is load-bearing — a default `Json` drops `"v":2`/`"role":"client"`/`"protocol_versions":["v2"]` and emits `"in_reply_to":null` instead of omitting it, both wire-breaking) plus top-level `base64StdEncode`/`base64StdDecode` (`java.util.Base64`, standard alphabet WITH padding = Go `base64.StdEncoding`) and `decodeServerStaticPubkey(qr)` which base64-decodes + `require(size==32)`, throwing a field-named, no-echo `IllegalArgumentException` on bad base64 or wrong length (AC #4 — stops a malformed X25519 key reaching #275's `Noise_IK` handshake). No per-model wrappers (the configured `MobileJson` + base64 helpers *are* the codec). `HelloClientPayload`/`QrPayload` override `toString()` to redact the device-pairing `token` to `***` (deterministic Logcat-leak safety net; serialization unaffected so the wire carries the real token; `equals`/`hashCode` deliberately NOT overridden). `java.util.Base64` is the one JVM-only seam (contained `expect/actual` if CMP lands; models stay portable). Reuses #272's `kotlinx-serialization-json` 1.8.1 — no new dependency. First live consumer is the [Noise_IK session](features/noise-ik-session.md) ([#298](codebase/298.md), split from #275 — consumes `Envelope`/`HelloClientPayload`/`HelloAckPayload` early-data via `MobileJson`); `payload_encrypted` was **resolved as not the session's** (its surface is raw bytes) — deferred to the application message set (#278), still dropped on decode today via `ignoreUnknownKeys`. Other consumers: #276 (relay WS client, frames `InnerFrameV2`), #277 (QR-string transport wrapper around `QrPayload`). **Application-payload DTOs** decode on top of `Envelope` — one file per `type` in `data/network/`, decode = the validate boundary; the first is `conversations` → domain `Conversation`-list ([#316](codebase/316.md)): object-wrapped array (not bare), timestamps decode to `kotlinx.datetime.Instant` at the DTO via `InstantIso8601Serializer` (so a malformed timestamp throws `IllegalArgumentException`, not `SerializationException` — #312 must catch both), wire-absent domain fields get documented list-tier defaults (never null-punned). [#317](codebase/317.md) added the second (`message` → domain `Message`): the payload IS the object (not wrapped); `WireRole` models only the domain-mappable roles so `system`/unknown is a free `SerializationException` at decode; the timestamp is the **envelope `ts`** parsed in the mapper (malformed → `IllegalArgumentException` at map, so two throw sites split decode/map); the non-wire `sessionId` is caller-supplied not defaulted. [#318](codebase/318.md) added the third (`conversation_created`/`conversation_updated` write-responses → domain `Conversation`): **one** `ConversationResponseDto` decodes both (identical field set, differ only in `cwd`↔`name` key order; kotlinx decodes by name), a bare object (like #317) but with both validations at decode (like #316 — `last_used_at` via `InstantIso8601Serializer`, mapper throw-free), reusing #316's four list-tier placeholder rule verbatim; consumed by the future create/promote mutation slices of `RemoteConversationRepository` (not #312). ktlint `standard:filename` named the single-class file `ConversationResponseDto.kt` (not the spec's `ConversationResponsePayload.kt`). [#346](codebase/346.md) added the first **outbound** wire types alongside the decode-only payloads: `SendMessagePayloadDto` (encode-only `send_message` request, mirrors #313's `BackfillSincePayloadDto`) + the generic correlated-reply `ErrorPayload{code,message,retryable}` / `RelayErrorException` (no `AckPayload` — `ack` is empty `{}`, never decoded; correlation is purely on `Envelope.inReplyTo`). Built inside the `sendMessage` mutation slice ([[phase4-request-encoders-live-in-mutation-tickets]]), reused by #347/#348. This also **corrects** #317's stale "send_message echo" framing — `send_message` returns an `ack`/`error`, never a `Message` echo to the sender ([[phase4-send-message-acks-not-message-echo]]). [#347](codebase/347.md) added the second outbound request encoder `CreateConversationPayloadDto` (its own file per the ktlint single-public-type rule [[ktlint-filename-rule-single-class]]; encode-only `create_conversation` request — `is_promoted` always `false` + optional `cwd`, `name` intentionally unmodeled since discussions are server-auto-named; a null `cwd` is omitted under `explicitNulls=false`, wire-compatible with the server's `*string` no-`omitempty` `Cwd` = server-assigned scratch cwd, #274 SSOT), reusing #346's `ErrorPayload`/`RelayErrorException` correlated-reply types and routing the typed `conversation_created` success reply through #318's `ConversationResponseDto.toConversation()`. [#348](codebase/348.md) added the third (and last #314) outbound request encoder `PromoteConversationPayloadDto` (its own file per the ktlint single-public-type rule [[ktlint-filename-rule-single-class]]; encode-only `promote_conversation` request — all three fields `conversation_id`/`name`/`cwd` **required/non-null**, contrast #347's optional `cwd`; the caller resolves a null `workspace` to the existing cwd before encoding so the wire always carries a concrete `cwd`, #274 SSOT), reusing #346's `ErrorPayload`/`RelayErrorException` correlated-reply types and routing the typed `conversation_updated` success reply through the **same** #318 `ConversationResponseDto` — so all three #314 mutations (`send_message`/`create_conversation`/`promote_conversation`) now ship live. [#359](codebase/359.md) added the fifth (and first **device-concern**) outbound request encoder `RegisterPushTokenPayloadDto` (its own file per the ktlint single-public-type rule [[ktlint-filename-rule-single-class]]; encode-only `register_push_token` request — all three fields `platform`/`token`/`device_name` **required/non-null**; `platform` a plain `String "fcm"` not an enum since an encode-only DTO models only what it sends; `token` a sensitive FCM credential held transiently and **never logged**; #275 SSOT), reusing #346's `sendAndAwaitReply`/`ErrorPayload`/`RelayErrorException` verbatim — the reply is a bare `ack`, so **no response DTO** is modeled. Consumed by `RemoteConversationRepository.registerPushToken`. [#374](codebase/374.md) added the **screen-snapshot exchange** in `SnapshotPayload.kt` — the first wire **pair in one file**: encode-only `RequestSnapshotPayloadDto` (`request_snapshot` `{conversation_id}`) + decode-only `ScreenSnapshotPayloadDto` (`screen_snapshot` `{conversation_id, text, ts}`), the parser-independent **floor** of ADR 025 safe-degradation (server SSOT pyrycode#617/#618). `ScreenSnapshotPayloadDto` is the **first decode-only payload with NO domain mapper** — terminal display text, the consumer reads `.text` directly (contrast #316/#317/#318, which all map to a domain type); `text` is modeled **verbatim** (no trim/normalize — decode fidelity is the floor's whole point, no-raw-bytes enforced server-side) and `ts` stays a plain **unparsed `String`** since no domain field consumes it (contrast #316/#318's `InstantIso8601Serializer` — refined rule: parse-at-decode a payload timestamp only when a domain field needs the instant). Two public types ⇒ ktlint `standard:filename` is silent, so it keeps the `SnapshotPayload.kt` name (contrast #318's single-class rename [[ktlint-filename-rule-single-class]]); no mobile-side type-string constants (envelope `type` inline at the consumer). Data shapes only — no repository/dispatch/trust decision; consumed by #375 (repository read), which owns the dispatch, the trust decision, and the no-content-logging invariant. [#401](codebase/401.md) added **capability negotiation** to both Hello payloads: `HelloClientPayload.capabilities` (defaults `["interactive"]`, rides the wire via `encodeDefaults` like `protocol_versions` — **test-proven**, not assumed) advertises; `HelloAckPayload.capabilities` (omitempty → `emptyList()` default) carries the daemon's echoed **intersection**; new top-level `internal const CAPABILITY_INTERACTIVE = "interactive"` is the SSOT token (no `@SerialName`). Surfacing-only — verbatim echo, **no** client-side re-intersection (that's the #385 gate's job); the [Noise_IK session](features/noise-ik-session.md) `.toSet()`s it onto `negotiatedCapabilities` → `PumpState.Open.capabilities` for the #385 decode gate / #395 stall gate. Wire SSOT pyrycode `protocol-mobile.md` § Capability negotiation; ADR 025.
- [App preferences](features/app-preferences.md) — `AppPreferences` wrapper over a shared `DataStore<Preferences>` (file `app_prefs`); typed `Flow<T>` reads + `suspend` setters for non-secret app state.
