`String.toChannelSlug()` pipeline is `lowercase()` → spaces to dashes → strip non-alphanumeric-or-dash → trim leading/trailing dashes → `ifEmpty { "channel" }` fallback (defensive against a name like `"!!!"` slipping past the dialog's non-blank gate). `ThreadScreen` renders the dialog as a fourth `Scaffold` sibling alongside [`WorkspacePicker`](features/workspace-picker.md), [`RenameDialog`](features/rename-dialog.md), and [`StatusSheet`](features/status-sheet.md): `state.saveAsChannelDialog?.let { dialogState -> SaveAsChannelDialog(initialName = dialogState.initialName, onSubmit = { name, workspace -> onOverflowEvent(ThreadEvent.SaveAsChannelSubmit(name = name, workspace = workspace)) }, onDismiss = { onOverflowEvent(ThreadEvent.SaveAsChannelDismiss) }) }` — **`?.let { dialogState -> … }` over `if (state.saveAsChannelDialog != null)`** because Kotlin's smart-cast doesn't extend through `if` to nullable-data-class-field reads (the `let` form gives the lambda a non-null receiver without a `!!` bang). `MainActivity` is **not** touched. `RecordingRepo` (the private test double in `ThreadViewModelTest.kt`) grew a real `promote(...)` body — gained `val promoteCalls = mutableListOf<Triple<String, String, String?>>()` and replaced the previous `TODO("not used")` with a recorder returning a minimal `Conversation(id, name, cwd = workspace ?: "", isPromoted = true, ...)`; tests assert on `promoteCalls` directly via `assertEquals(listOf(Triple(...)), repo.promoteCalls)` (the `Triple` shape matches the existing `renameCalls: MutableList<Pair<String, String>>` posture, keeping the test double uniform). The existing `onOverflowEvent_otherCases_doNotCallArchive` iteration list **was not extended** — `SaveAsChannel` was never in that loop (#204's no-op arm didn't need that coverage). Tests: 11 Compose UI tests in `androidTest/.../SaveAsChannelDialogTest.kt` mirroring `RenameDialogTest`'s scaffold (`title_field_label_radios_and_buttons_render`, `field_prefills_with_initial_name`, `dedicated_radio_selected_by_default`, `save_enabled_with_seeded_name`, `save_disabled_when_input_cleared`, `save_disabled_on_whitespace_only_input`, `save_enabled_with_non_blank_trimmed_input`, `selecting_scratch_radio_updates_selection`, `tapping_save_with_dedicated_invokes_onSubmit_with_trimmed_name_and_dedicated_choice`, `tapping_save_with_scratch_invokes_onSubmit_with_scratch_choice`, `tapping_cancel_invokes_onDismiss_without_invoking_onSubmit`) — `assertIsSelected()` / `assertIsNotSelected()` resolve to the *row* node (because `Modifier.selectable(role = Role.RadioButton)` is on the row), not the `RadioButton` glyph; `performTextReplacement(...)` carries forward from #141's lesson (the field opens pre-seeded so `performTextInput` would append rather than replace). Four new unit tests on `ThreadViewModelTest.kt` pin the VM dispatcher (`onOverflowEvent_saveAsChannel_setsDialogStateWithSeededName`, `_saveAsChannelSubmit_dedicated_callsPromoteWithSlugPath` — asserts `promoteCalls == listOf(Triple("seed-channel-personal", "Investment Strategy Review", "pyry-workspace/channels/investment-strategy-review"))`, `_saveAsChannelSubmit_scratch_callsPromoteWithNullWorkspace` — asserts `Triple(id, name, null)`, `_saveAsChannelDismiss_clearsDialogWithoutPromote`). **No new dependencies, no Koin changes, no `gradle/libs.versions.toml` edits, no `MainActivity.kt` edits, no data-layer changes** (the `promote(conversationId, name, workspace = null)` contract has been on `ConversationRepository` since the data-layer earlier slices; this is its second production caller after [#78](codebase/78.md)'s discussion-list long-press promote). Second downstream of the four per-item follow-ups [#252](codebase/252.md) left as `Unit` arms (after #141 wired `Rename`); the other two remain open ([#208](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/issues/208) for `ChangeWorkspace`, Channel Info sheet host for `ChannelInfo`, not-yet-ticketed for `NewSession`).
- [Device static keystore](features/device-static-keystore.md) — per-server device static (Noise `s`) keypair custody ([#291](codebase/291.md)) at `data/crypto/`, the phone's long-term `Noise_IK`-initiator identity. Portable `interface DeviceStaticKeyStore` (zero `android.*`) — `suspend loadOrCreate(serverId): DeviceStaticKeyPair` (raw 32-byte X25519 pub+priv) + `suspend publicKey(serverId): ByteArray?` (plaintext-mirror read, no Keystore op) — over the Android-bound `KeystoreDeviceStaticKeyStore(dataStore)` impl. Mechanism (a) **wrap-at-rest** ([ADR 0006](decisions/0006-keystore-wrap-at-rest-device-static-key.md)): the raw private scalar (`SecureRandom()`) is AES-256-GCM-wrapped under a shared, hardware-backed, uid-scoped `AndroidKeyStore` key (alias `pyrycode.device_static_wrap`, per-encrypt Keystore-generated IV — closes the GCM nonce-reuse footgun) and stored as `base64(iv‖ct)` in the existing app-private `app_prefs` DataStore; the 32-byte public key is mirrored in plaintext for fast reads. Two pref namespaces — `pyrycode.device_static.<serverId>` (priv blob) vs `pyrycode.device_static_pub.<serverId>` (pub mirror) — **diverge at the fixed char right after the common stem (`.` vs `_`)** so no attacker-influenced server-id (QR pairing payload) can collide a priv blob with another server's pub mirror (the architect's one inline MUST-FIX; never reintroduce a `.pub` *suffix*). Both written in one `dataStore.edit{}` (no half-keypair). Public key derived **through noise-java** (`Noise.createDH("25519")`→`setPrivateKey`→`getPublicKey`) so the mirror is byte-identical to what [#275](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/issues/275)'s `Curve25519DHState` produces. Idempotent under a single `Mutex` (deterministic "never silently regenerates"); a decrypt failure (tampered blob / wrap key gone / restore-to-new-device) **throws `DeviceStaticKeyException` — does NOT regenerate** (a fresh key changes the device identity → server close `4426`; re-pair is a future ticket), the load-bearing security property, negatively tested. No key material in logs/exception messages. `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)`; one Koin binding (`bind DeviceStaticKeyStore::class`), no new dependency (Keystore + `javax.crypto` framework, noise-java vendored). Tested by one instrumented file (`androidTest/.../KeystoreDeviceStaticKeyStoreTest`, 6 `@Test`s — real `AndroidKeyStore` needs a device; fresh-instance-over-same-DataStore is the app-restart proxy). Consumed by #275 (initiator's local static key); sibling of the [wire layer](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) ([#273](codebase/273.md)).
- [Paired server store](features/paired-server-store.md) — encrypted-at-rest custody of the **reconnect credential record** ([#294](codebase/294.md)) at `data/crypto/`: the four values the phone re-presents to reconnect after process death. Portable `interface PairedServerStore` (zero `android.*`) — `suspend load(): PairedServer?` + `suspend save(record)` — over the Android-bound `KeystorePairedServerStore(dataStore)` impl. `@Serializable data class PairedServer(serverId, token, relayUrl, serverStaticPublicKey)`, all four `String`, stored/reloaded **byte-faithfully** (only the outer JSON document is encrypted; field Strings pass through untouched, so the hex token, base64-STD pubkey with `+`/`/`/`=`, and relayUrl with its `/v1/client` path survive byte-identical for [#275](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/issues/275)/[#276](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/issues/276)). The record's **only** override is a redacting `toString()` → `PairedServer(serverId=…)` (the bearer `token` never reaches the auto-generated `toString` → no Logcat leak); `equals`/`hashCode`/`copy` stay generated. **Second consumer of [ADR 0006](decisions/0006-keystore-wrap-at-rest-device-static-key.md) wrap-at-rest** — same AES-256-GCM-under-`AndroidKeyStore` envelope as [#291](codebase/291.md), with three deliberate divergences: (1) a JSON document (not raw key bytes) as plaintext; (2) a graceful `null`-returning `load()` — absent **and** undecryptable/corrupt both resolve to `null` (→ re-pair), **never throws** (the **inverse** of #291's throw-don't-regenerate; safe because the record is QR-re-fetchable → no identity-drift risk); (3) a **dedicated** wrap-key alias `pyrycode.paired_server_wrap`, distinct from #291's `pyrycode.device_static_wrap` (no cross-purpose key reuse). Single-record simplifications: one fixed pref key `pyrycode.paired_server` (server-id lives *inside* the encrypted blob → none of #291's attacker-influenced key-collision surface) and **no `Mutex`** (one atomic `dataStore.edit{}`, concurrent saves last-writer-wins). `load()`'s graceful catch set is the **specific** `{GeneralSecurityException, IOException, IllegalArgumentException}` (covers `AEADBadTagException`, `SerializationException`, `Base64.decode`) — **never `Exception`/`Throwable`**, so `CancellationException` propagates; an explicit `blob.size <= 12 → null` guard forestalls the un-caught `IndexOutOfBoundsException` a truncated blob would throw on the IV split. `load()` uses a **non-creating** `KeyStore.getKey` lookup so a read never mints a key; `save()` throws `PairedServerStoreException` (cause-class + operation, never bytes) on Keystore/IO failure. `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)`; one Koin binding (`bind PairedServerStore::class`), no new dependency. Tested by one instrumented file (`androidTest/.../KeystorePairedServerStoreTest`, 7 `@Test`s, adversarial sample record — real `AndroidKeyStore` needs a device; fresh-instance-over-same-DataStore is the app-restart proxy). **Live as of #295** — `MainActivity` gates the start destination on `load() != null` and the Scanner placeholder persists a stub via `save()`; the old `pairedServerExists` boolean was removed, making this store the sole live paired-state source (the #275/#276 credential consumers stay dormant). Mirrors the [device static keystore](features/device-static-keystore.md) ([#291](codebase/291.md)).
- [Noise_IK session](features/noise-ik-session.md) — the **phone's leg of the Phase 4 encrypted transport** ([#298](codebase/298.md)) at `data/network/`: a `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s` **initiator** wrapping the vendored `noise-java` ([ADR 0004](decisions/0004-vendor-noise-java-crypto.md)) that drives the handshake, recovers the server's `conn_id`, splits into transport ciphers, and exposes AEAD `encrypt`/`decrypt` over byte arrays — the leg that keeps the relay **untrusted and content-blind**. Proven end-to-end by the 2026-05-29 spike (interop with the Go `flynn/noise` responder, zero byte-level debugging). Portable, `android.*`-free: `class NoiseIkSession(localStaticPrivateKey, remoteStaticPublicKey, token, clientInfo)` with lifecycle `NEW ─writeInit()→ AWAITING_RESP ─readResp()→ ESTABLISHED ─close()→ CLOSED` (`encrypt`/`decrypt` ESTABLISHED-only; wrong-order calls → `IllegalStateException`, runtime crypto/protocol failures → `NoiseSessionException`; not resumable). Load-bearing, spike-proven facts (all test-pinned): (1) **no prologue** — `start()` mixes the empty prologue, never `setPrologue` (a non-empty prologue diverges the handshake hash → Go responder rejects msg1); (2) **initiator does NOT swap on `split()`** — `sender`=encrypt-out, `receiver`=decrypt-in (the spike's "one high-risk line"; responder mirror-swaps); (3) **`ad = null` on every transport op**, ct = pt + 16 (Poly1305 tag) — empty AD is load-bearing per `protocol-mobile.md`; (4) the **token rides inside the encrypted `hello` early-data**, never a plaintext header. `hello`/`hello_ack` framed by [#273](codebase/273.md)'s `Envelope`/`HelloClientPayload`/`HelloAckPayload`, **always via `MobileJson`** (a default `Json{}` would drop defaulted fields → wire-breaking). Inputs from two producers: local `s` from [`DeviceStaticKeyStore`](features/device-static-keystore.md) ([#291](codebase/291.md)), remote `rs` + token from [`PairedServerStore`](features/paired-server-store.md) ([#294](codebase/294.md)). `NoiseSessionFactory.create()` (`withContext(ioDispatcher)`) resolves both stores into a ready-to-handshake session and collapses every setup failure into one `NoiseSessionException` (cause preserved; `not paired` / `invalid server static key` re-validated to 32 bytes / `device static key unavailable`), zeroing its private-key copy after the constructor copies it into the DH state; does **not** open a socket. Key hygiene: no `Log`/`Timber`, category-only exception messages, `handshake.destroy()` post-`split()` (safe — `split()` *forks* independent transport ciphers) bounds the device-key RAM window to the handshake; **plain `class`, deliberately not a `data class`** (retains `token` until `writeInit` → a generated `toString` would leak it to a crash frame). Belt-and-suspenders **deterministic nonce-reuse net**: **every method touching shared cipher/handshake state is `@Synchronized`** on the instance monitor — the handshake pair `writeInit`/`readResp` ([#497](codebase/497.md)), `encrypt`/`decrypt`/`close`, and re-key `writeRekeyInit`/`readRekeyResp` ([#303](codebase/303.md)) — so a same-direction race (silently catastrophic under ChaCha20-Poly1305) is excluded **and** a teardown `close()` firing mid-handshake can't wipe `handshake` (device static-key copy + handshake secrets) out from under an in-flight `writeInit`/`readResp` (the [#497](codebase/497.md) use-after-wipe); single-pump is the contract, the lock is the floor, and a reflective test pins `Modifier.isSynchronized` over the full seven-method set. Two Koin singletons — `NoiseClientInfo(Build.MODEL, BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME)` resolved Android-side + injected as strings, `NoiseSessionFactory(get(),get(),get())` (first registered consumer of both crypto stores) — no new dependency. JVM-tested (`test/.../NoiseIkSessionTest` 30 `@Test` — 16 handshake/transport + 5 capability (#401) + 9 re-key — against an in-test `flynn/noise` `TestResponder` mirror with a re-key leg + `NoiseSessionFactoryTest` 4 `@Test` with faked stores). Transport surface is **byte-array in / byte-array out**: WS framing/`InnerFrameV2`/frame-size cap + `RemoteConversationRepository` (#276/#278) and `payload_encrypted` (#278) are out of scope. The in-place **re-key mechanism** (`writeRekeyInit`/`readRekeyResp` — fresh IK handshake vs the same pinned `rs`, empty early-data, atomic CipherState swap + old-key wipe under the existing `@Synchronized` lock, **fail-RETAIN & retryable**, device static `s` taken by parameter so the bounded RAM window holds across every rotation; initiator mirror of Go `pyrycode` #453) landed in [#303](codebase/303.md); its 1-hour-timer / `rekey_request` triggers landed in [#304](codebase/304.md) on the [Noise session pump](features/noise-session-pump.md) ([#309](codebase/309.md)), which re-loads `s` per re-key via the new `NoiseSessionFactory.reloadDeviceStaticKey()`. [#401](codebase/401.md) added `val negotiatedCapabilities: Set<String>` — the granted capability set surfaced from `hello_ack`, mirroring `connId`'s throws-until-established discipline (private nullable backing field written once in `readResp`); `parseHelloAck` now returns the whole `HelloAckPayload` (one decode, two fields) and `readResp` `.toSet()`s `capabilities` at the post-MAC boundary (wire `List` → membership `Set`, dedup once). Surfacing-only, carried onto the [pump](features/noise-session-pump.md)'s `PumpState.Open.capabilities`. Aligns with pyrycode-side ADR 024 (Noise_IK for mobile E2E).
- [Relay WebSocket transport](features/relay-ws-transport.md) — the **single-connection WS leg of the Phase 4 transport** ([#306](codebase/306.md), split from #301) at `data/network/`: `OkHttpRelayTransport` dials the paired relay's `/v1/client` endpoint and carries bare [`InnerFrameV2`](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) JSON **text** frames both ways via `MobileJson`. It is the wire under the [Noise_IK session](features/noise-ik-session.md) but **transport-only and content-blind** — it (de)serializes `InnerFrameV2` ↔ JSON text and **never inspects a frame's `data`** (Noise ciphertext, authenticated one layer up). Portable, `android.*`-free: `interface RelayTransport { val inbound: Flow<InnerFrameV2>; val events: Flow<TransportEvent>; connect(); send(frame): Boolean; close() }` + `sealed interface TransportEvent { Up; Down(code: Int?, reason: String?, cause: Throwable?) }` (the interface is justified now — three named consumers fake it: the sibling supervisor, #278, #302). **Single-use lifecycle** `NEW ─connect()→ DIALING ─Up→ UP ─Down→ terminal`: one `connect()` = one socket, terminal on the first `Down`, then the streams complete and the instance is spent (a supervisor discards it and builds a fresh one to reconnect — Noise ephemerals are per-handshake, so no resumable state and the reconnect state machine stays out of this layer). **Dials `/v1/client` verbatim** — path appended as a literal on the URL **string** (`base.trimEnd('/') + "/v1/client"`, never `addPathSegment` which would `%2F`-encode the slash), with the spike's `wss→https`/`ws→http` `replaceFirst` scheme conversion (OkHttp's `HttpUrl` rejects `ws`/`wss`); **never `/v2/client`** (relay serves `/v1/*` only; `v2` is the inner-frame `"v":2`). Headers from `PairedServer` + `NoiseClientInfo`: `X-Pyrycode-Server` (serverId), `X-Pyrycode-Token` (token — relay **ignores** it under v2, real token is in #298's encrypted hello), `User-Agent` (clientVersion), and `X-Pyrycode-Device-Name` (deviceName from `Build.MODEL`) **only when `isNotBlank()`** (omitted for empty **and** whitespace-only; sourced from `NoiseClientInfo`, not `PairedServer`). **`Down` enriches the ticket's `Down(cause)` with `code` + `reason`** so the supervisor and #308 can tell a `4401` auth-reject (→ re-pair) from a network blip (→ retry) — OkHttp already hands these over. **Channel-backed hot single-consumer streams** (`receiveAsFlow()`, not `SharedFlow`): `inbound` fed by `trySendBlocking` so a full buffer **blocks the OkHttp reader thread → TCP flow-control → never a silent drop** (a drop breaks the upstream Noise nonce sequence); on `Down` both channels `close()` and buffered frames drain before completion. **Bad data → uniform `Down`, bad call order → throw**: a malformed `relayUrl` / header-injection attempt is caught in `connect()` → `Down(IllegalArgumentException)` (does **not** throw); only `connect()`-twice throws `IllegalStateException`. Untrusted-relay defence at the single `onMessage` crossing — post-receive size guard (`MAX_INBOUND_FRAME_CHARS = 131_072` = 128 KiB; the inbound cap #298 deferred to the WS layer), then decode; a malformed/oversized/binary frame → local `close()` (`1002`/`1009`/`1003`) + a single terminal `Down` (tears down rather than drops). The terminal `Down` runs **exactly once** via `terminated.compareAndSet`, closing the race across `onClosed`/`onFailure`/local-violation/`close()`. The transport **owns no coroutine/scope** (OkHttp owns threads; channels bridge to `Flow`). Security-load-bearing `defaultClient()` lives + is unit-asserted here (the singleton binding is the supervisor's): `connect`/`write` 15 s, `pingInterval` 20 s (the WS liveness mechanism), **`readTimeout`/`callTimeout` = 0 deliberately** (both kill a long-lived WS; `pingInterval` provides liveness instead), `MODERN_TLS` + `CLEARTEXT` with legacy `COMPATIBLE_TLS` dropped, and **no `HttpLoggingInterceptor`** (would dump the token); no `Log`/`Timber`/`println` anywhere, `Down.reason` category-only; **no relay-cert pinning** (intentional — the trust anchor is the QR-pinned Noise `rs`, verified E2E in #298). The dial seam is the `WebSocket.Factory` constructor param (production passes the shared client; tests drive `MockWebServer` through the same `newWebSocket`). **Ships dormant** — no Koin binding (same pattern as [`PairedServerStore`](features/paired-server-store.md)/`NoiseSessionFactory`); `FakeConnectionStateSource` stays bound. JVM-tested (`test/.../OkHttpRelayTransportTest` 14 `@Test` against an in-process `MockWebServer` WS upgrade, bridging real OkHttp threads with `runBlocking { withTimeout(...) }` — not `runTest` virtual time; a `ClosingServerListener` base echoes the peer close, or `shutdown()` hangs ~5 s; the binary + oversize-frame reject branches were the two tests [#522](codebase/522.md) added). One `testImplementation`-only dep (`mockwebserver`, version-shared with `okhttp`). On the OkHttp engine ([ADR 0005](decisions/0005-okhttp-websocket-engine.md) — first production reference); spike-proven (vault *"Phase 4 — Noise Client Spike Findings"*). Consumed-by: the [reconnect supervisor](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) ([#307](codebase/307.md), **landed** — drives `connect()`/`close()` + collects `events`, swaps the `ConnectionStateSource` binding); still to come — the #309 Noise pump (`inbound`), #278, #302, #308.
- [Relay reconnect supervisor](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) — the **Phase 4 reconnect-policy layer** ([#307](codebase/307.md), split from #301) at `data/network/`: `RelayConnectionSupervisor` is the **real `ConnectionStateSource`** that turns a series of single-use [#306](codebase/306.md) sockets into a continuously-available connection, swapping the Koin binding `FakeConnectionStateSource → RelayConnectionSupervisor` in `AppModule`. It drives the transport's `connect()`/`close()` and collects **only** its `events` (`Up`/`Down`) — **never `inbound`** (that single-consumer stream is #309's Noise pump). Maps live socket state onto the 4 [`ConnectionState`](features/connection-state.md) cases: `Connecting` while dialing, `Connected` on `Up`, a **per-second `Reconnecting(secondsRemaining)` countdown** sub-cap, `Offline` at the 30 s cap (keeps redialing ~every 30 s — **not** terminal). Backoff is the wire-spec capped-exponential `1/2/4/8/16/30 s` with **±20 % jitter** (`0.8 + nextDouble()*0.4`, **exactly one `nextDouble()` per interval** so a seeded `Random` replays deterministically), resetting `attempt` to 0 only after a connection has been **stable ≥ 60 s** (an `AtomicBoolean` flipped by a child timer, read after `cancel()` — the architect's one concurrency MUST-FIX, since `cancel()` is no memory barrier under a multi-threaded dispatcher). Reconnect = **discard the spent transport + ask `RelayTransportFactory` for a fresh one** (a `fun interface`; #306 sockets are single-use, Noise ephemerals per-handshake → no resumable state). `retry()` collapses any pending backoff via `withTimeoutOrNull(ms) { retrySignal.receive() }` racing a CONFLATED channel, **never throws**, and does **not** reset `attempt` (anti-reconnect-storm). **Benign-unpaired**: no stored `PairedServer` → no dial, stays `Connected` (banner hidden), tap-to-retry re-checks + stays idle. Two cross-sibling **architect's calls** (documented, not defaulted): **(A)** `Connected` = **socket-open**, not Noise-session-open (gating on #309 would couple them; out of scope); **(B)** transport-instance ownership via `currentConnection: StateFlow<RelayTransport?>` — the non-precluding seam that lets the layer-up coordinator (#302/future) hand the same connection's `inbound` to a fresh #309 pump. **App-wired but dormant until driven** — the [lifecycle connection driver](features/lifecycle-connection-driver.md) ([#302](codebase/302.md), **landed**) now calls `connect()`/`close()` across foreground/background edges; bound as the concrete type too so the driver can resolve it via `get<RelayConnectionSupervisor>()`. No UI change (the [`ConnectionBanner`](features/connection-banner.md)/`ThreadViewModel` consume the unchanged interface); `FakeConnectionStateSource` kept (only its binding removed — still used by tests). JVM-tested (`test/.../RelayConnectionSupervisorTest` 8 `@Test`, `runTest` virtual clock + a fake `RelayTransport` + seeded `Random`; settle event transitions with `runCurrent()`, reserve `advanceTimeBy`/`advanceUntilIdle()` for waits). No new dependency. On the OkHttp engine ([ADR 0005](decisions/0005-okhttp-websocket-engine.md)) via #306; code review **PASS** ([PR #310](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/310)). **[#391](codebase/391.md) retyped the single source of truth** to the relay-leg [`RelayLinkStatus`](features/relay-link-status.md) (the four cases **plus** `DaemonAbsent`) exposed as `val relayStatus: StateFlow<RelayLinkStatus>`, and **derived** the legacy `ConnectionState` from it per-collector (`observe() = state.map { it.toConnectionState() }`, `DaemonAbsent → Offline`) so every consumer + the exhaustive-`when` banner stay untouched; the `#308 seam` Down arm now branches the relay's `4404 "no server"` close into a steady `DaemonAbsent` (relay reachable, no daemon registered) on the **same** backoff schedule (so it flips off the instant a daemon registers), while every other code — `4401`/`1006`/`1000` + a `null` dial failure — retries as before. No DI change (#392 fetches `relayStatus` off the concrete supervisor like `currentConnection`); code review **PASS** ([PR #393](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/393)). **[#496](codebase/496.md) fixed a close-then-connect race in the loop's `finally`.** `connect()`/`close()` are `@Synchronized` and can't interleave with each other, but a cancelled loop's `finally` runs **outside** that lock (cancellation is cooperative), so if a fresh `connect()` had already started a loop that reached `Up` and published its transport, the old loop's unconditional `liveConnection.value = null` wiped the **new** loop's live connection — `currentConnection` read `null` over an open socket, so [`RelayRepositoryCoordinator`](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md) never built a pump and the connection was **silently dead until the next reconnect** (background→quick-foreground or push-wake trigger; `USE_RELAY_REPOSITORY`-only). Fix: the `finally`'s clear is now an atomic identity **compare-and-clear** — `liveConnection.update { if (it === transport) null else it }` (atomic because the scope is multi-threaded `Dispatchers.Default`) — so a loop can only ever retract the transport it itself published; `transport.close()` stays unconditional (every loop releases its socket) and `close()`'s own synchronous clear stays unscoped. One production line; the diff's bulk is a deterministic interleaving test (a `GatedRelayTransport` holds the old loop's cancellation in a `NonCancellable` gate past the new loop's publish, then releases — a naive single-`runCurrent()` interleaving is false-green). Same race **class** as [#391](codebase/391.md)'s sibling [#493](codebase/493.md), one layer down; `security-sensitive`, availability-positive. Code review **PASS** ([PR #517](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/517)). **[#498](codebase/498.md) drained a stale `retrySignal` at the top of `backoff()`.** `retry()` writes into a **CONFLATED** channel whose sole job is to collapse a backoff wait happening *now*; issued while **`Connected`** (loop inside `events.collect`, no receiver parked) the `Unit` buffered with nothing to collapse, and the *next* drop's first `collapsibleWait` `receive()` consumed that stale signal and re-dialled instantly — shortening a backoff that should have waited its full jittered interval (LOW/self-correcting, `USE_RELAY_REPOSITORY`-only). Fix: `while (retrySignal.tryReceive().isSuccess) {}` at the **top** of `backoff()`, before all three wait branches (daemonAbsent/sub-cap/cap), drops exactly the pre-drop signals; a `retry()` arriving *during* a wait lands after the drain so it still collapses (AC#2), and `retry()` itself is untouched (AC#3). Placement is load-bearing — **not** inside `collapsibleWait` (which runs per-second in the sub-cap countdown and would swallow an in-progress retry). One production statement; a narrow multi-threaded-dispatcher gap (a `trySend` racing between `events.collect` completing and the drain) is knowingly left undefended per Evidence-Based Fix Selection, non-reproducing on the single-threaded test dispatcher. Pure timing (**not** security-sensitive); code review **PASS** ([PR #519](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/519)). **[#499](codebase/499.md) gave the deliberately-idle situations their own `RelayLinkStatus.Idle` case.** The supervisor had overloaded one `Connected` for both a live socket (transport `Up`) and three genuinely idle states — the initial pre-`connect()` seed, the unpaired `null`-read branch, and `close()` — harmless while the banner (idle = absence of banner) was the only consumer, but once the Settings status line began reading a *positive* claim off the same value it dragged the relay leg to a **false green "Connected" while unpaired**. Fix: a new `data object Idle` (no relay text → no-log contract) at the three idle sites, and the initial `state` seed is now `Idle`. Both consumers are exhaustive `when`s, so the compiler forced each to route it — `toConnectionState()` maps `Idle → Connected` (banner stays hidden, unchanged) while `toLegVisual()` maps `Idle → Down`/"Not connected" (honest Settings label): the divergence is the whole fix. Live socket `Up` still reads `Connected`/green; `Connecting`/`Reconnecting`/`DaemonAbsent`/`Offline` unchanged. XS, **not** security-sensitive; code review **PASS** ([PR #520](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/520), zero findings).
- [Relay link status](features/relay-link-status.md) — `data/model/RelayLinkStatus.kt` ([#391](codebase/391.md), split from #389): the **relay-leg** half of the two-part connection status — a sealed class mirroring [`ConnectionState`](features/connection-state.md)'s four cases (`Connected`/`Connecting`/`Reconnecting(secondsRemaining)`/`Offline`) **plus** `DaemonAbsent` for the relay's `4404 "no server"` close (relay reachable, no daemon registered behind it) — a state the legacy single-signal `ConnectionState` cannot express. Portable (no `android.*`), beside `ConnectionState.kt`, its own file per the ktlint single-public-class rule. Produced by the [`RelayConnectionSupervisor`](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) as its single hot source of truth (`val relayStatus: StateFlow<RelayLinkStatus>`, a plain public property on the concrete supervisor — no interface, no DI change); the legacy `ConnectionState` is **derived** from it per-collector via the `internal RelayLinkStatus.toConnectionState()` mapper (`DaemonAbsent → Offline`, the nearest legacy banner meaning). **Why a new type not a fifth `ConnectionState` case**: adding `DaemonAbsent` there would break the [`ConnectionBanner`](features/connection-banner.md)'s exhaustive `when` (no `else`) at compile time and force premature banner UI — Strangler Fig instead (new leg model alongside the old, old derived). `security-sensitive`: the relay-controlled close `code` is untrusted and crosses into trusted state at exactly one integer `== 4404` comparison; `null`/other codes cannot masquerade as `DaemonAbsent`; `DaemonAbsent` is a static object carrying no relay text (no-log contract holds). **Consumer ([#392](codebase/392.md), landed)** (`blockedBy #391`) — zips this relay leg with the pyrycode-session-readiness leg into the combined `{relay, pyrycode}` banner model; today only the *derived* `observe()` is consumed (`relayStatus` ships ahead of its consumer). **[#499](codebase/499.md) added a sixth case, `Idle`** — the deliberately-not-dialing state (initial pre-`connect()` seed, unpaired branch, `close()`), split out of the overloaded `Connected` so the two derived surfaces can **diverge**: `toConnectionState()` maps `Idle → ConnectionState.Connected` (banner hidden — idle is not an error) while the Settings line's `toLegVisual()` maps `Idle → Down`/"Not connected" (non-green, honest), closing the false green on the relay leg while unpaired/idle. A `data object` carrying no relay text (no-log contract, like `DaemonAbsent`); named for the *situation* not a cause (`close()` was paired, the seed predates pairing). The supervisor's initial `MutableStateFlow` seed is now `Idle`. Code review **PASS** ([PR #520](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/520)).
- [Connection status](features/connection-status.md) — `data/model/ConnectionStatus.kt` + `PyrycodeLinkStatus.kt` ([#392](codebase/392.md), split from #389): the **combined two-part** `ConnectionStatus { relay, pyrycode }` model the Settings connection-status line (#390, `blockedBy #392`) consumes — holds both legs **verbatim** so each is shown independently (a green relay dot beside a handshaking pyrycode dot is now an expressible, honest state) — plus the new **pyrycode leg** `PyrycodeLinkStatus` (3-case sealed `Handshaking`/`Connected`/`Down`; `Connected` is a `data object` dropping `connId`) layered on top of #391's relay [`RelayLinkStatus`](features/relay-link-status.md). The pyrycode leg is the **honest** `relay → daemon` end-to-end session-readiness signal: it reaches `Connected` **only** once the [Noise pump](features/noise-session-pump.md) reaches `PumpState.Open` (handshake complete, after `readResp` authenticates `noise_resp`) — **never on bare socket-up** (the relay leg's `Connected` means *socket-open*, not Noise-session-open), and not at all between connections — closing the false green that bit live testing **2026-06-08**. **No server polling / heartbeat / status endpoint** (explicit constraint): readiness is purely the handshake completion the pump already surfaces. **Derived + published in the [`RelayRepositoryCoordinator`](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md)** (it owns the connection-scoped pump), as **pure derivation, no new mutable status state**: a private `activePumpFlow: MutableStateFlow<ManagedSessionPump?>` mirrors the live pump (written in lock-step with `mutableRepository` inside the non-suspending `onConnection`/`teardownActive` critical section); a private `pyrycodeStatus = activePumpFlow.flatMapLatest { it?.state ?: flowOf(null) }.map { it.toPyrycodeLinkStatus() }` (the same `flatMapLatest`-over-a-live-child idiom [`StableConversationRepository`](features/stable-conversation-repository.md) uses — tracks the **current** pump across reconnects with no carryover); and the public `connectionStatus: StateFlow<ConnectionStatus>` = `combine(relayStatus, pyrycodeStatus).stateIn(scope, Eagerly)` on the coordinator's existing scope. The mapping `internal fun PumpState?.toPyrycodeLinkStatus()` (sibling to #391's `toConnectionState()`) is total over `PumpState` + the no-pump `null`: `null`/`Closed → Down`, `Handshaking → Handshaking`, `Open → Connected`; it **discards `Open.connId` and `Closed.cause`**, so the **no-log / no-leak contract is structurally enforced** (no relay/crypto-derived string reaches the status surface). Wiring: `relayStatus` added as a coordinator ctor param off the concrete supervisor (`get<RelayConnectionSupervisor>().relayStatus`, like `currentConnection`) — **no new Koin binding**; #390 fetches `get<RelayRepositoryCoordinator>().connectionStatus`. Portable (no `android.*`), each its own file (ktlint single-public-class rule). Init-order gotcha: `stateIn(Eagerly)` runs at property init, so the derived props are declared **after** `scope` + `activePumpFlow` (else construction-time NPE). `security-sensitive` — untrusted-relay-driven pump state crosses into trusted status at exactly one total mapping that discards every relay/crypto string; `Connected` reachable only via `Open`, so a hostile relay **cannot inflate** the signal. Code review **PASS** ([PR #394](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/394), zero findings).
- [Connection status line](features/connection-status-line.md) — the stateless two-part `● Relay   ● Pyrycode` component ([#397](codebase/397.md), the build slice of #390) that renders the combined [`ConnectionStatus`](features/connection-status.md) `{relay, pyrycode}` model ([#392](codebase/392.md)), closing the **no-positive-connection-indicator** gap (`Connected` was conveyed only by the *absence* of the [`ConnectionBanner`](features/connection-banner.md)) that the daemon-not-on-relay bug surfaced on **2026-06-08**. Load-bearing logic is a **pure mapper** (`RelayLinkStatus`/`PyrycodeLinkStatus.toLegVisual() → (ConnectionLegCategory{Up,InProgress,Down}, label, contentDescription)`, exhaustive `when` no `else`, **no Compose import** → JVM-unit-tested per case, all 8); the M3 token is resolved at one separate `@Composable ConnectionLegCategory.color()` site (`Up→colorScheme.success` / `InProgress→.warning` / `Down→.error` — **no hardcoded hex** in the component, only in `Color.kt`). **AC#2**: `RelayLinkStatus.DaemonAbsent → Up` (green, label "Reachable") — relay reachable, the missing daemon is the *pyrycode* leg's story; the green-relay + red-pyrycode compound **is** the diagnostic. The component is a `Row` of two `StatusLeg`s = **[8.dp dot][name][state label]** with `clearAndSetSemantics` (one TalkBack phrase per leg); **not colour-only** (name + textual label legible without colour), stable inputs → **skippable**. Light/Dark previews cover all four AC combos (incl. `DaemonAbsent`+`Down`). Introduces the green [`success` token](features/success-color.md). Code review **PASS** (zero blocking, [PR #399](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/399)). **Live wiring shipped in [#398](codebase/398.md)** (slice B of #390, `blockedBy #397`, code review PASS, [PR #400](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/400)): the [coordinator](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md)'s hot `connectionStatus` is forwarded verbatim onto [`SettingsViewModel`](features/settings-viewmodel.md) (no `stateIn` re-wrap), injected via Koin off the concrete coordinator (no new binding), collected lifecycle-aware at the `MainActivity` Settings host, and this component rendered under the **Server** row of the Connection section in the [Settings screen](features/settings-screen.md) — both legs now update live (flag-off debug builds honestly read `Offline / Down`). Design landed 2026-06-08 (Settings frame `17-2`, node `90-4`); residual micro-spacing is a non-blocking visual-polish follow-up. **[#499](codebase/499.md) added the `Idle` relay-leg mapping** — `RelayLinkStatus.Idle.toLegVisual() → (Down, "Not connected", "Relay: not connected")`, rendering through the existing `Down → colorScheme.error` path (no new token, no hardcoded hex). It **diverges** from the banner mapper's `Idle → Connected` (hidden): same `Idle` value, non-green Settings label — the "share the `Down` category with `Offline`, diverge on the label" mirror of the `Connected`/`DaemonAbsent` `Up`-sharing pattern — so the Settings relay leg stops reading a false "Connected" while unpaired/idle. Sixth relay case; the per-case unit test is `relayIdle_mapsToDown_notConnected`. Code review **PASS** ([PR #520](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/520)).
- [Lifecycle connection driver](features/lifecycle-connection-driver.md) — the **Phase 4 transport-lifecycle layer** ([#302](codebase/302.md), split from #276) at `lifecycle/`: `LifecycleConnectionDriver` decides **when** the relay connection is alive, sitting on top of the [reconnect supervisor](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) ([#307](codebase/307.md)) which decides **how**. A stateless `DefaultLifecycleObserver` on `ProcessLifecycleOwner` (the whole-app foreground/background signal, **not** config changes): **`onStart` (foreground) → `connect()`**, **`onStop` (background) → `close()`** (tear down the socket + stop the loop so **no on-drop backoff fires while backgrounded** — and `close()` → idle `Connected`, so a deliberate background close **never** surfaces as `Offline`/`Reconnecting`), **`onPushWake()` → `connect()`** (the payload-free in-process entry the future FCM service calls; identical to the foreground path — safe foreground-or-background because `connect()` is idempotent, so the driver needs **no fg/bg bookkeeping**). Drives a **narrow portable seam** `RelayConnectionController { connect(); close() }` declared in `data/network` beside its only implementor (the supervisor gains the interface + `override` ×2, **bodies unchanged**) so the platform `lifecycle` package depends **inward** and the driver test needs only a recording double — and the data-parameter-free surface means no relay-/push-controlled data can be injected through it. **No new state type, no transport, no Noise** — the [`ConnectionState`](features/connection-state.md) surface is untouched; this layer changes only *which* state is published across lifecycle edges, via the existing supervisor. All AC-4 guarantees (non-overlap, unpaired-idle, close≠`Offline`) are **delegated to the supervisor** — the driver's whole contract is "each background pairs with `close()`, each foreground with `connect()`". **Eager Koin wiring, no `PyryApp` change**: `single(createdAtStart = true) { LifecycleConnectionDriver(get<RelayConnectionSupervisor>(), ProcessLifecycleOwner.get().lifecycle).also { it.start() } }` constructs + registers the observer at `startKoin` (main thread, `Application.onCreate`), reusing the dormant supervisor singleton (upcast) and staying resolvable so a future FCM service can `get()` it. **Out of scope (named):** re-closing a push-opened background connection (needs the push payload → FCM ticket) and FCM token registration; `onPushWake()` → `connect()` (not `retry()`) is the right call for push-from-idle, switch to `retry()` only if a future "force-immediate during a pending backoff" requirement appears. **One new dep** (`androidx.lifecycle:lifecycle-process` for `ProcessLifecycleOwner` — **not** transitive via `lifecycle-runtime-ktx`, at the existing `lifecycleRuntimeKtx` ref). Zero logs. JVM-tested (`test/.../LifecycleConnectionDriverTest` 7 `@Test`, no instrumentation/Robolectric/clock — a recording `FakeRelayConnectionController` + a `LifecycleRegistry.createUnsafe` owner driven via `handleLifecycleEvent`, the zero-dep lifecycle test seam that skips the main-thread check); code review **PASS**.
- [Noise session pump](features/noise-session-pump.md) — the **Phase 4 encrypted-session layer** ([#309](codebase/309.md)) at `data/network/`: `NoiseSessionPump` is the missing foundational glue that binds the content-blind [relay WS transport](features/relay-ws-transport.md) ([#306](codebase/306.md)) and the byte-array [`Noise_IK` session](features/noise-ik-session.md) ([#303](codebase/303.md)/[#298](codebase/298.md)) into **one** running encrypted session — the **mobile mirror of the Go binary's `V2SessionManager`** (pyrycode #446 dispatch / #433 IK wrapper), executing `protocol-mobile.md` § Connection lifecycle → Phone **steps 3–6**. Surfaced during #304's architect review as the gap that fell through the #301 → #306/#307 split. Portable, `android.*`-free; **2** exported types: `class NoiseSessionPump(transport, sessionFactory, dispatcher = Default, handshakeTimeoutMs = 10_000)` + `sealed interface PumpState { Handshaking; Open(connId); Closed(cause) }`. **`start()`** (single-use; a second call throws) launches **one** coroutine that drives the handshake: `factory.create()` → send a `noise_init` frame (the encrypted `hello`+token ride sealed inside `writeInit()`, never a plaintext header) → `withTimeoutOrNull(handshakeTimeoutMs) { inbound.first() }` (the protocol-step-4 10 s `noise_resp` deadline; closes the silent-hang hole left by #306's deliberate `readTimeout = 0`) → require `type == "noise_resp"` → `readResp()` → `state = Open(connId)` → `inbound.collect { onOpenFrame }`. **`state: StateFlow<PumpState>`** is both the handshake-completion signal (consumers `filterIsInstance<Open>().first()` before sending) and the terminal lifecycle signal (`cause == null ⟺ clean Down/close()`). **One sequential collector for the connection's lifetime** — the handshake `.first()` and the open-state `.collect()` are the *same* consumer of #306's single-consumer, `receiveAsFlow()`-backed `inbound` (sequential re-collection *distributes*, never duplicates; the channel buffers the gap → no frame lost); **never split across two coroutines** (would fight #306's contract; Go built it as one `V2SessionManager`). **Open-state dispatch is a clean `when (frame.type)`**: `noise_msg` → `decrypt(base64Decode(data))` → `MobileJson.decodeFromString<Envelope>` → emit on `inbound` **only after a successful decrypt + parse** (a forged/tampered/replayed frame fails AEAD → never reaches a consumer); `else` → teardown — the ordered encrypted stream **cannot skip a frame**, so any unknown type / base64 / decrypt / parse failure tears the session down rather than dropping. **[#304](codebase/304.md) landed the re-key triggers on this pump**: a 1-hour timer armed off `Open` (re-based per re-handshake) + an inbound `rekey_request` envelope (intercepted in the `noise_msg` branch, **not** forwarded, payload not decoded) both funnel into one `Mutex`-serialised + `@Volatile`-coalescing `initiateRekey()` that re-loads the device static `s` (via the new `NoiseSessionFactory.reloadDeviceStaticKey()`, zeroed in a suspension-free `finally`) and drives #303's `writeRekeyInit`; the re-key `noise_resp` routes through a **new `noise_resp` branch** to `readRekeyResp` (atomic swap, no `rekey_ack`), while a stray or MAC-failed `noise_resp` (rotated `rs` / MITM) tears down. **[#495](codebase/495.md) added a bounded re-key response watchdog**: `initiateRekey` arms a finite `rekeyRespTimeoutJob` (`scope.launch { delay(rekeyRespTimeoutMs); teardown(…) }`, default `REKEY_RESP_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000` ms — the Go WS-4426 mirror, pyrycode #450) **right after** the re-key `noise_init` is sent and **inside** the send `try`, cancelled by the completing `noise_resp`; on timeout it funnels to the existing `teardown` so a single **lost** `noise_resp` can no longer wedge `rekeyInFlight` true forever (running indefinitely on un-rotated keys) — #307 rebuilds a fresh pump on fresh keys. Recovery is teardown, **not** same-session retry (`writeRekeyInit`'s `check(pendingRekey == null)` can't be re-satisfied without `session.close()`); the watchdog **only** calls `teardown`, never re-arming the 1-hour timer, and job-cancellation alone suffices (the single-re-key-in-flight invariant means watchdog A is cancelled by resp A before re-key B arms watchdog B — no generation counter). This supersedes #304's deliberate "no phone-side reply timeout" deferral, which the Cross-Repo Code Review 2026-07-03 refuted for the lost-resp case. **Outbound `send(Envelope): Boolean`** returns `false` unless `Open` (or if a racing teardown closed the session → `encrypt`'s `IllegalStateException` caught → `false`, no throw, no nonce consumed); else under `synchronized(outboundLock)` `encrypt` → `base64Encode` → `transport.send(noise_msg)`. The lock spans the **whole** encrypt→enqueue pair because the AEAD nonce is an implicit per-session counter the wire doesn't carry — wire order must == nonce order or the receiver MAC-fails (the session's own `@Synchronized` covers `encrypt` alone, not the gap to `transport.send`). **`inbound: Flow<Envelope>`** is a hot single-consumer `Channel.BUFFERED` `receiveAsFlow()` — lossless + in-order (the collector `send`s suspending → backpressure all the way down to TCP). **One CAS-idempotent, non-suspend `teardown(cause)`** for every trigger (handshake fault / fatal frame / transport `Down` / `close()`): `state = Closed` → channel close → `session.close()` (**wipes keys**) → `transport.close()` (idempotent; active on the fatal path so the supervisor sees `Down`) → `scope.cancel()` **last** (key-wipe never behind a cancellable suspension). **`Down` detection = `inbound` completing** (#306's terminal-`Down` guarantee) → `teardown(null)`; the pump **never reads `events`** (that's #307's) — keeping both single-consumer contracts clean. **Code-independent sibling of [#307](codebase/307.md)** over the *same* connection (supervisor owns `events` + the socket lifecycle, pump owns `inbound` + the Noise session; **no blocker**); a fresh pump per fresh connection via the supervisor's `currentConnection` seam. **Ships dormant** — no Koin binding, no consumers (grep-confirmed); #302 is to build a pump per connection but that seam is **unwired** (and #302 landed before this pump existed). No logs; every failure surfaces only via `Closed(cause)`, a `NoiseSessionException` with a category-only message (no `frame.data`/plaintext/token). JVM-tested (`test/.../NoiseSessionPumpTest` 14 `@Test`, `runTest` virtual clock + a `Channel`-backed `FakeRelayTransport` whose `connect()` `error()`s + a real IK `TestResponder` + a `NoiseSessionFactory` pinned to it; the no-leak collector launches as a **foreground** child of the test scope, not `backgroundScope` — those aren't drained by `advanceUntilIdle()`). **No new dependency.** On the OkHttp engine ([ADR 0005](decisions/0005-okhttp-websocket-engine.md)) + vendored noise-java ([ADR 0004](decisions/0004-vendor-noise-java-crypto.md)); `security-sensitive`, § Security review **PASS** (three findings delegated to code-review all confirmed); code review **PASS** ([PR #325](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/325)), 2 non-blocking NITs (the encrypt-before-enqueue nonce-gap — a `false` on an `Open` session means the session is **spent**, not retry-the-envelope, an awareness note for #278's `send`; and a `readResp` bare-`catch` consistency nit). Consumed by: **[#304](codebase/304.md)** (re-key triggers — **landed**, above); still to wire — **#278** (`RemoteConversationRepository` — `inbound` + `send`) and **#302** (per-connection construction). **[#401](codebase/401.md) added `val capabilities: Set<String> = emptySet()` to `PumpState.Open`** (defaulted → the 4 existing `Open(connId)` construction/assertion sites stay green, and `RelayRepositoryCoordinator.toPyrycodeLinkStatus()`'s `is Open` match is unaffected; `drive()` passes `session.negotiatedCapabilities`) — surfacing the negotiated set through the already-exposed `state` flow, read via `CAPABILITY_INTERACTIVE in open.capabilities`. Surfacing-only (decode gate #385 / stall gate #395 consume it).
- [Relay repository coordinator](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md) — the **Phase 4 connection-scoping layer** ([#351](codebase/351.md), split from #349) at `data/repository/`: `RelayRepositoryCoordinator` observes [`RelayConnectionSupervisor.currentConnection`](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) (#307) and, per live transport, starts a fresh [Noise pump](features/noise-session-pump.md) (#309) over it and constructs a [`RemoteConversationRepository`](features/remote-conversation-repository.md) (#312/#313/#329/#346) against the pump on a connection-scoped child scope, publishing the live repo — or `null` between connections — on `currentRepository` (the seam the **#352** stable facade consumes so ViewModels never re-resolve across connection churn). **The slice every prior Phase 4 read/mutation slice deferred to as "the owner (#279/#302)"** of the connection scope + the `SessionPump` binding. Adds `ManagedSessionPump : SessionPump` — the coordinator's lifecycle view (`start()`/`close()`) over `SessionPump`'s `inbound`/`send` data view (Interface Segregation) — and makes `NoiseSessionPump : ManagedSessionPump` (purely additive, no behaviour change; AC #1). A single **non-suspending** `onConnection(transport?)` collector runs sequentially: tear down the active connection (cancel the child scope, **then** `pump.close()` — keys wiped; the pump's own scope is not a child of the child scope), then if non-null build a fresh pump + child scope + repository. **Crypto invariants** (deterministically tested): every pump-reference drop calls `close()` (wipes session ciphers + device-static copy) across all three teardown transitions; a fresh single-use pump per connection (no `Noise_IK` ephemeral / AEAD-nonce reuse — reconnect carries over no pump, projection, or in-flight state); never collects the transport's single-consumer `inbound`/`events`. The non-suspending body makes each emission atomic w.r.t. a racing `close()`. DI-wired as a `single(createdAtStart = true)` mirroring [`LifecycleConnectionDriver`](features/lifecycle-connection-driver.md); **does NOT bind `ConversationRepository`** — the [Fake](features/conversation-repository.md) stays the default until the **#350** flag-gated swap (AC #5). JVM-tested (7 `@Test` over a fake `ManagedSessionPump` + the **real** repository so the `list_conversations` / `backfill_since` read paths run end-to-end; `StandardTestDispatcher` + `runCurrent()`, `coordinator.close()` per test). No logs (only outward signal is `currentRepository`). **[#365](codebase/365.md)** added the connect-time FCM push-token re-registration: `ManagedSessionPump` gains `val state: StateFlow<PumpState>` (`NoiseSessionPump.state` gains `override`, no behaviour change) so the coordinator can await `Open`; two **defaulted** ctor params `deviceName: String = ""` + `pushToken: suspend () -> String? = { null }` (wired live in `AppModule` to `NoiseClientInfo.deviceName` = `Build.MODEL` / `AppPreferences.pushToken.first()`); and `onConnection` — still **non-suspending** — `childScope.launch`es a hook that awaits `pump.state.first { Open || Closed }`, reads the token (`null` ⇒ no-op, dormant until #361 stores one), and calls the **concrete** repo's [`registerPushToken`](features/remote-conversation-repository.md) ([#359](codebase/359.md)) **once per connection** (structural exactly-once; the state machine never revisits `Handshaking`), swallowing failure but re-throwing `CancellationException`, never logging the token. Threading the live `deviceName` **closes #359's `device_name: ""` defer** (an empty name forks the server's `(platform, token, device_name)` dedup triple). 5 new tests (AC #1–4 + pre-Open-`Closed` boundary). Code review **PASS** ([PR #354](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/354), no findings; #365 [PR #367](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/367), one optional NIT).
- [Relay diagnostic log](features/relay-log.md) — **debug-gated, redaction-safe diagnostic logger for the relay transport** (#521, `data/network/RelayLog.kt`, split from #500): `object RelayLog` with `d`/`i`/`w(message: () -> String)` + `redactConnId(connId): String`. Safe **by construction** on two axes — (1) **cannot reach release**: emit is gated on an explicit `if (enabled)` where `enabled` defaults to the compile-time `BuildConfig.DEBUG` constant (`false` in release), a real build check **not** R8 log-level stripping (which the `Log.i`/`Log.w` 2026-07-03 near-miss defeated; no UI toggle, no prod setter); (2) **cannot assemble a sensitive value in release**: the message is a `() -> String` lambda invoked only *inside* the gate, so the string is never built. `redactConnId` = full 256-bit BLAKE2s digest of the UTF-8 `conn_id` → **first 4 bytes** → 8-char lowercase hex (`^[0-9a-f]{8}$`), deterministic (same id → same token, correlate within a session), revealing **zero** live-id bytes; a fixed-width digest of an arbitrary-length string can never equal its input, so "not the full input" holds for short/empty too (why hashing beats `connId.take(8)` prefix-truncation — no edge-case guard). The **4-byte width is a *correlation* parameter, deliberately NOT the ≥8-byte MITM [static-key fingerprint](features/static-key-fingerprint.md)** (an *authentication* requirement) — don't "align to 8". Methods are **non-`inline` on purpose** (keeps the `internal` `enabled`/`sink` test seams clean; the runtime gate, not inlining, is the load-bearing guarantee). Object KDoc carries the MUST-NOT-log contract (relay host/URL, pairing token, raw key material, full `conn_id`, payloads) + safe-to-log allowlist (event type, redacted id, pump state name, close code, capability *names*). Reuses the vendored `Blake2sMessageDigest` ([ADR 0004](decisions/0004-vendor-noise-java-crypto.md)); no new dep, no consumer change. **Facility only** — the four "Emits no logs" relay files ([supervisor](features/relay-reconnect-supervisor.md) / [coordinator](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md) / [pump](features/noise-session-pump.md) / [transport](features/relay-ws-transport.md)) stay untouched; adoption is a future ticket. `android.util.Log` in `data/` is sanctioned (platform-logging custodian). Unit-tested via a capturing `sink` seam because real `android.util.Log` throws on plain JVM (no Robolectric). Per-ticket notes: [codebase/521.md](codebase/521.md).
- [Stable conversation repository](features/stable-conversation-repository.md) — the **stable indirection** ViewModels hold so relay connection churn never invalidates an injected repository ([#352](codebase/352.md), split from #349) at `data/repository/`: `StableConversationRepository` is one process-lifetime singleton whose object identity never changes, delegating every call to whichever connection-scoped [`RemoteConversationRepository`](features/remote-conversation-repository.md) the [coordinator](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md) (#351) publishes on `currentRepository` (`StateFlow<ConversationRepository?>`), switching transparently as connections come and go. Overrides **all 14** interface members (incl. the 3 defaulted ones) in two shapes: the 4 **cold reads** (`observeConversations`/`observeMessages`/`observeLastMessage`/`recentWorkspaces`) share one `switchToLive(whenAbsent, select)` helper = `currentRepository.flatMapLatest { repo -> repo?.let(select) ?: flowOf(whenAbsent) }` — cancel-old-on-switch drops the prior connection's projection (a reader never re-observes a previous connection's data after reconnect, AC #2) and `null` falls back to the empty projection (`emptyList()`/`null`), resuming on the next connection (AC #3); the 10 **one-shots** snapshot `private val live get() = currentRepository.value ?: throw IllegalStateException(NOT_CONNECTED)` once at entry and delegate verbatim — a wired mutation, a throwing stub (`archive`/`rename`/… → `UnsupportedOperationException`), and a wired error all propagate unchanged (AC #4). **Not-connected = `IllegalStateException`**, matched deliberately to the type `RemoteConversationRepository.sendAndAwaitReply` throws on a not-`Open` pump (`RemoteConversationRepository.kt:262`) so a caller catches **one** type whether the connection was absent at call time or dropped mid-flight. Owns **no scope**, launches no coroutine, stores no mutable state beyond the injected flow — a pure transformer. DI-registered as a lazy `single { StableConversationRepository(get<RelayRepositoryCoordinator>().currentRepository) }` resolvable by its own type; **does NOT bind `ConversationRepository`** — the [Fake](features/conversation-repository.md) stays the default until the **#350** flag-gated swap (`AppModule.kt:57` untouched, AC #5). Disconnected renders as the empty projection (no distinct connection-status surface yet — a follow-up). No logs (the `NOT_CONNECTED` string carries no ids/bodies). `security-sensitive`, § Security review **PASS** (cross-connection leak precluded by the `flatMapLatest` switch + #351's distinct-repo-per-connection). JVM-tested (8 `@Test` driving a `MutableStateFlow<ConversationRepository?>` directly — no coordinator, no pump — `StandardTestDispatcher` + `runCurrent()`, the key test a ghost-push to the cancelled repoA surfacing nowhere). Code review **PASS** ([PR #358](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/358), one NIT). Consumed by **#350** (the flag-gated Fake↔Remote binding swap + ViewModel rewiring).
- [LiteralScreenViewModel](features/literal-screen-viewmodel.md) — state-management half of the manual "show the literal screen" feature ([#378](codebase/378.md), split from #372; render surface + thread entry-point + Koin registration are sibling **#379** `blockedBy #378`); thread-scoped VM at `ui/conversations/thread/LiteralScreenViewModel.kt` that orchestrates one `ConversationRepository.requestScreenSnapshot(conversationId)` read (shipped [#375](codebase/375.md)) and hoists it as MVI `state: StateFlow<LiteralScreenUiState>` (`Loading` initial → `Content(text)` / `Error(reason)`) + `onEvent(LiteralScreenEvent.Request/Retry)`, both driving one `load()` with latest-request-wins (`loadJob?.cancel()`) and `catch (CancellationException) { throw e }` **before** the generic catch (load-bearing — else cancelling the prior job paints a spurious `Error`). `reasonFor` maps `IllegalArgumentException`→`UnknownConversation` / `RelayErrorException`→`ServerError` / `IllegalStateException`→`NotConnected` (disjoint — `RelayErrorException : Exception`, not `RuntimeException`) + defensive `else`→`ServerError`; the closed `LiteralScreenError` enum keeps the error state free of any server string. `security-sensitive`: snapshot text is server-originated, held verbatim (no trim/parse/sanitize) and never logged — two-layer net (no `Log.*`/`println` + redacting `Content.toString()` → `<redacted N chars>`, mirrors `ScannerUiState.Decoded`, test-pinned); only the non-sensitive `conversationId` is read from `SavedStateHandle` and the text never round-trips through it (no disk persistence across process death). The `Loading`/`Content` snapshot is the always-available, parser-independent floor of pyrycode ADR 025 safe-degradation (pyrycode#596). No `AppModule`/Koin change here — deferred to the surface slice [#381](codebase/381.md) (now shipped — see [LiteralScreenSurface](features/literal-screen-surface.md)) + entry-point #382
- [LiteralScreenSurface](features/literal-screen-surface.md) — render half of the manual "show the literal screen" feature ([#381](codebase/381.md), split from #379 on the surface-vs-entry seam → #381 surface + DI / **#382** entry + nav `blockedBy #381`); stateless `LiteralScreenSurface(state, onEvent, onBack, modifier)` at `ui/conversations/thread/LiteralScreenSurface.kt` renders the merged [`LiteralScreenViewModel`](features/literal-screen-viewmodel.md) ([#378](codebase/378.md)) hoisted `LiteralScreenUiState` — `Loading`→centered `CircularProgressIndicator`, `Content`→**verbatim** snapshot (`Text(text, fontFamily = FontFamily.Monospace, softWrap = false)` in a both-axes-scrollable `Column`, **never** through [`MarkdownText`](features/markdown-text.md), no pre-processing, **no `SelectionContainer`** = no clipboard exfil), `Error`→per-`reason` string resource + Retry `Button` (closed `LiteralScreenError` enum → fixed copy, never a server string) — under a shared `TopAppBar` (title + back; refresh `IconButton` only in `Content`, fires `Retry`). `LaunchedEffect(Unit) { onEvent(Request) }` fires `Request` once per open (VM never auto-fetches; recording-lambda tests see `[Request, Retry]`). `security-sensitive` render-edge confidentiality: introduces the repo's **first `FLAG_SECURE`** via a private `SecureScreen()` `DisposableEffect(Unit)` (add-on-enter / clear-on-dispose) driven by a null-safe `tailrec Context.findActivity()` walk — no-ops under `createComposeRule` (non-Activity host) so security tests run device-free; plus no `Log.*`/`println`, no `rememberSaveable`/`SavedStateHandle` of the text (only the non-sensitive `rememberScrollState`). VM's redacted `Content.toString()` never undone (renders `state.text`, never `"$state"`). DI: `viewModel { LiteralScreenViewModel(get(), get()) }` in `AppModule` makes the VM **resolvable** (`get()` → `SavedStateHandle` + `ConversationRepository`); per-conversation instance scoping (no cross-conversation text bleed) deferred to **#382**. 7 instrumented tests (`createComposeRule`, `ThreadOverflowMenuTest` idiom); markdown-bypass test-pinned; monospace not asserted (not queryable via the test API). **Design owed** (Figma N/A) — no `@Preview` (non-blocking code-review SHOULD-FIX), visual polish + preview retrofit with the frame (#343 precedent). Code review **PASS** ([PR #383](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/383))
- [Live-session events](features/live-session-events.md) — the **v2 structured-stream decode seam** ([#385](codebase/385.md), split from #368): wire → typed events for the five **binary → phone** envelopes (`turn_state`, `assistant_delta`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`, `turn_end`) the daemon emits **only** to a phone whose `interactive` capability was echoed in `hello_ack` ([#401](codebase/401.md), pyrycode#607/#616). Three layers: five `@Serializable internal data class` DTOs in `data/network/InteractivePayloads.kt` (required non-null fields, snake_case `@SerialName` Go-interop, decode through [`MobileJson`](features/mobile-protocol-v2-wire-layer.md) only — a missing/wrong-typed field fails the strict decode = fail-closed) → `toEvent()` mappers → the public **portable** `sealed interface LiveSessionEvent` in `data/model/LiveSessionEvent.kt` (five subtypes named 1:1 with the wire `type`, common `conversationId`, nested `TurnState.Phase{Thinking,Responding,Idle}` enum so the file keeps one public top-level type per the [[ktlint-filename-rule-single-class]]; free-form `text`/`inputSummary`/`resultSummary`/`stopReason` carried **verbatim**, never trimmed/parsed/sanitized). Surfaced as a hot `val liveSessionEvents: SharedFlow<LiveSessionEvent>` (`replay=0`, `extraBufferCapacity=64`, `DROP_OLDEST` → `tryEmit` **infallible + non-blocking** so a slow consumer never back-pressures the shared `pump.inbound` collector) on the **concrete** [`RemoteConversationRepository`](features/remote-conversation-repository.md) — **not** the interface (the accepted [`registerPushToken`](features/remote-conversation-repository.md) #359 pattern; facade reachability for UI consumers is downstream). Rides the **single existing** inbound collector (one grouped demux arm, no second subscription). **AC#2 gate**: `CAPABILITY_INTERACTIVE in negotiatedCapabilities()` checked **before** decode, where `negotiatedCapabilities: () -> Set<String>` is a **lazy supplier** (defaulted `{ emptySet() }`) wired by the [coordinator](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md) from `(pump.state.value as? PumpState.Open)?.capabilities` — lazy because the repo is built while the pump is still `Handshaking` (a captured snapshot would be empty forever), and `pump.state.value` is a non-suspending read (preserves `onConnection` cancellation-atomicity). **AC#3** unrecognized/absent `turn_state.state` and **AC#4** malformed envelope both **drop one envelope** (nullable `toEvent()` for unknown `state`; one `try/catch (IllegalArgumentException)` — `SerializationException ⊂` it — for malformed) without tearing down the stream; no `Phase.Unknown` member. **Decode-only** — correlation/accumulation/turn-lifecycle/rendering are consumer slices (#386 thinking indicator, #387 tool timeline, #337 live assistant text, all unblocked). `security-sensitive`: single trust boundary behind the authenticated Noise channel, **no payload logging** (load-bearing — text/tool-summaries may be sensitive; grep-confirmed zero `Log`/`println` incl. the drop branch), bounded buffer = DoS-resistant, `seq:Int` never sizes an allocation, connection-scoped flow = no cross-connection leak. Pattern note: a value-mapped wire field that must **tolerate** unknown values uses a **nullable mapper**, not a strict serialized enum (contrast `WireRole`). Code review **PASS**, zero findings ([PR #404](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/404)).
- [Turn-state thinking flag](features/turn-state-thinking-flag.md) — the **data/ViewModel half of the thinking indicator** ([#406](codebase/406.md), split from #386; the stateless composable + placement is the sibling UI slice #407, which adds no UI here). Closes the reachability gap that kept #385's decoded `turn_state` from the thread VM: the typed [`LiveSessionEvent`](features/live-session-events.md) stream lives on the **concrete**, connection-scoped `RemoteConversationRepository.liveSessionEvents` — **not** the `ConversationRepository` interface. Two hops, both reusing the `registerPushToken` (#359/#365) / `connectionStatus` (#392/#398) precedent: (1) the [coordinator](features/relay-repository-coordinator.md) surfaces a **generic, reconnection-surviving** `val liveSessionEvents: Flow<LiveSessionEvent>` = `activeRemoteRepo.flatMapLatest { it?.liveSessionEvents ?: emptyFlow() }` over a new **private** `activeRemoteRepo` mirror written lock-step with `mutableRepository`/`activePumpFlow` in the non-suspending `onConnection`/`teardownActive` critical section (cold — events have no current value, so no `stateIn`; full stream not an `isThinking` projection so #387/#337 reuse it); (2) `ThreadViewModel` takes it as a **defaulted** trailing ctor param (`liveSessionEvents: Flow<LiveSessionEvent> = emptyFlow()` — keeps the fake-backed graph + existing 4-arg tests inert, AC #5) and reduces it to a **sibling** `val isThinking: StateFlow<Boolean>` = `liveSessionEvents.mapNotNull { thinkingTransition(it) }.stateIn(viewModelScope, WhileSubscribed(5_000), false)`. `thinkingTransition` routes by `conversationId` first (other conversations → `null`, AC #3) then maps the phase: `Thinking → true`; `Responding`/`Idle`/`TurnEnd → false`; non-phase `AssistantDelta`/`ToolUse`/`ToolResult` → `null`. `mapNotNull`+`stateIn` = latest-wins-with-hold (no-emission events retain the prior value); `false` initial covers both "no event yet" (AC #2) and the inert default (AC #5). A **sibling `StateFlow`, not a `ThreadUiState` field** — mirrors `connectionState` (transient, connection-scoped, taken as a separate `ThreadScreen` param), keeping the max-arity-5 `state` combine zero-touch. Wired off the concrete coordinator singleton at the `AppModule` `ThreadViewModel` factory — **no new Koin binding**. No UI, no new exported types, 3 prod files. Known accepted: `WhileSubscribed(5_000)` over `replay=0` can read a stale `true` on re-foreground (matched to `connectionState`/#395 posture; #407 follow-up if jarring). Code review **PASS**, zero blocking ([PR #408](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-mobile/pull/408)).
- [Thinking indicator](features/thinking-indicator.md) — the **UI half of the thinking indicator** ([#407](codebase/407.md), split from #386; the data/ViewModel half is the sibling [turn-state thinking flag](features/turn-state-thinking-flag.md), #406). A new **stateless** `fun ThinkingIndicator(isThinking: Boolean, modifier: Modifier = Modifier)` at `ui/conversations/components/` renders [`ThreadViewModel.isThinking`](features/turn-state-thinking-flag.md) as a small indeterminate M3 `CircularProgressIndicator(size(16.dp), strokeWidth = 2.dp)` + `Text` "Thinking…" (`bodySmall`/`onSurfaceVariant`) row, with a merged-`semantics` content description (`cd_thread_thinking` = "Agent is thinking", AC #5). `if (!isThinking) return` makes it a **pure function of the hoisted boolean** — no `remember`/side-effect/`ThreadUiState` field, mirroring [`ConnectionBanner`](features/connection-banner.md)'s early-return show/hide idiom; ships light+dark previews on the [`EmptyThreadState`](features/empty-thread-state.md) template. [Placed](features/thread-screen.md) as the **final child of the thread content `Column`, outside the empty/populated `if/else`** (`ThreadScreen.kt:221`), so one call site surfaces it at the foot (bottom/most-recent edge, `reverseLayout = true`) in **both** the populated and the **empty-thread** case (thinking precedes the first assistant text, AC #2) with no `hasMessages` gate. Threaded through the stateless `ThreadScreen` as a **defaulted** hoisted boolean (`isThinking: Boolean = false`, after `modifier`, `:74`) — a flat `State` sibling to `connectionState`, **not** a `ThreadUiState` field — collected at `MainActivity` via `vm.isThinking.collectAsStateWithLifecycle()` (`:349`). - [Stall state](features/stall-state.md) — the per-conversation **thread-observable stall** `Boolean` ([#395](codebase/395.md), split from #373; the visible reaction is sibling #396): decode the capability-gated v2 `stall` control envelope (`{conversation_id}` only — server SSOT pyrycode#638/#639, tui-driver #141 v1.3.0) into `observeStall(conversationId): Flow<Boolean>`, **on the [`ConversationRepository`](features/conversation-repository.md) interface with a `flowOf(false)` default** so it reaches the thread through the [`StableConversationRepository`](features/stable-conversation-repository.md) facade (AC #4) — the deliberate **inverse** of #385's concrete-only `liveSessionEvents` (state-needed-through-the-facade vs events-the-thread-doesn't-need). Rides the **single existing** `pump.inbound` collector in [`RemoteConversationRepository`](features/remote-conversation-repository.md): a fourth connection-scoped `stalledConversations: MutableStateFlow<Set<String>>` (membership = stalled, single-writer → onset/clear never race), **onset** on a new `TYPE_STALL` arm, **clearing** folded into the existing #385 live-session arm — the wire is **onset-only** (no `stall_cleared`), so recovery is **inferred mobile-side** from the next *successfully decoded* forward-progress [`LiveSessionEvent`](features/live-session-events.md) (all five clear, incl. `turn_state: idle`/`turn_end`; a malformed/unrecognized-state live envelope decodes to `null` and does **not** clear). Both arms gated on `CAPABILITY_INTERACTIVE in negotiatedCapabilities()` (fail-closed, defence-in-depth over the server fan-out gate); `decodeStall` drops a malformed `{conversation_id}` without tearing down the collector (AC #3). `observeStall = stalledConversations.map { id in it }.distinctUntilChanged()` (1:1 with `observeLastMessage`); facade `switchToLive(false)` + fresh repo per connection (#351) → a stall never survives a reconnect. `StallPayloadDto` in `InteractivePayloads.kt`, **no `toEvent()` mapper** (state, not an event). `security-sensitive` — narrowest interactive boundary (no free-form text; surfaces only a `Boolean`; no payload logging), architect self-review PASS.
